# GT1-04-01 selected-account storage

Implemented the account-scoping portion of native history in the existing
`GrokSessionStorage` and shared `AgentSessionStorage` interface. Production scope
is exactly those two files. Native qualification and deterministic verification
remain **not-run**; no capability or whole parity row is promoted.

## Implementation and boundaries

- `listSessions(projectPath, sshConfig, accountDir)` selects the account home.
  Paginated listing forwards `SessionListOptions.configDir` and uses the
  existing base pagination helper. The same resolved sessions directory flows
  through cwd enumeration, session enumeration and metadata reads.
- `readSessionMessages(..., { configDir }, sshConfig)` reads that account;
  `getSessionPath(projectPath, sessionId, sshConfig, accountDir)` constructs a
  path in that same root. The shared interface adds only optional fields and
  trailing arguments, retaining existing call positions.
- `searchSessions(projectPath, query, mode, sshConfig, accountDir)` resolves an
  explicit selection once, then delegates through an immutable account-bound
  Grok reader to inherited search. Both metadata and message loading use that
  root. No registered-instance mutation, duplicate search algorithm or changes
  to other providers are needed. The optional constructor scope also reaches
  list, paginated list, read and path methods.
- Phase 02's canonical `resolveAgentAccountDir()` replaces the two local-only
  Grok home helpers. Explicit roots never consult host `GROK_HOME`; explicit
  blank/whitespace roots select the provider default. Omitted scope retains
  local host-environment/default behavior. Local tilde/relative paths use the
  canonical resolver; SSH roots stay remote and use POSIX joins without local
  home expansion or environment fallback.
- A missing selected root yields the existing empty list/read/search results.
  Path lookup remains a best-effort synchronous path in the selected root; it
  does not assert file existence or search another account. No fallback to an
  installed default account is added.

The reference Codex/Copilot account storage, shared base and storage interface,
phase 01 source/schema evidence, phase 02 account rules and phase 03 native
identity evidence were reviewed. Existing summary, transcript and usage parsing,
native IDs, macOS cwd aliases, remote FS operations, bounded concurrency and error
reporting are unchanged. Candidate package 1.0.70 and the pinned public source
`2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` are historical inputs, not a new
binary/schema qualification claim. No installed account files, provider calls,
credentials or live remote hosts were accessed.

## Implementation and review

- Extended the existing Grok path validator to accept opaque native IDs instead
  of requiring UUIDs. Reject empty names, dot/dot-dot components, raw slash or
  backslash and NUL before directory joins. IDs remain literal, including percent
  escapes; they are never sanitized or decoded into a different session name.
- Apply validation to discovered session names, direct lookup before filesystem
  access, metadata loading and synchronous path lookup. Validate raw cwd folder
  components before strict percent decoding; reject malformed escapes, decoded
  NUL and decoded dot/dot-dot path components before joins. Encoded slashes,
  spaces, Unicode, apostrophes and either percent-hex case remain supported.
  Path lookup returns null for malformed Unicode instead of throwing URIError.
- Use the validated directory's ID and decoded cwd for returned history identity.
  Stale/malicious summary identity cannot redirect later search/read operations
  to another session or project. Removed the now-unused summary identity fields
  from its consumed-fields interface.
- Preserve local macOS `/private/var`, `/private/tmp` and `/private/etc` aliases.
  SSH filtering compares remote paths without assuming these aliases exist on
  the unknown remote OS. Keep trailing-slash cleanup and correctly include
  descendants when the requested project is the POSIX root.
- Continue using `path.posix.join()` for SSH and `readDirRemote()`,
  `readFileRemote()` and `statRemote()`. Their canonical
  `shellEscapeRemotePath()` handles remote tilde expansion and quoted path text;
  the adapter never resolves remote paths through local filesystem calls.
  Remote directory symlinks retain the shared helper's existing behavior. This
  is lexical path validation, not a new realpath/symlink containment guarantee.
- Preserve `mapWithConcurrency()` with the existing remote limit of six session
  workers and sequential reads inside each worker. Expected remote missing or
  inaccessible paths retain honest empty/null results; unexpected SSH failures
  retain warning/Sentry context. Reuse `isExpectedSessionReadError()` at all
  local catches instead of repeating the narrower three-code checks.

Independent source review confirmed the account/root flow and join boundaries.
It also identified the decoded `/project/../other` filter bypass; the final
change rejects dot components so that malformed cwd cannot masquerade as a
project descendant. No installed account files, credentials, native provider
turns or live SSH endpoints were accessed. Images analyzed: **0**.

branch match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base

## Authored coverage and isolation

- Reused `sessionFiles()` with a POSIX join option and extended the existing
  `mockRemoteSession()` into an exact-path fake tree. Unknown files and accounts
  return misses rather than borrowing another fixture's content.
- Remote default/blank/custom/tilde roots, spaces, Unicode and apostrophes keep
  list, paginated list, read, search and path lookup in the selected account.
  A host-local GROK_HOME decoy cannot change remote scope; an absent selected
  account cannot fall back to available default history.
- Malformed cwd components, percent escapes, invalid UTF-8, decoded NUL and dot
  traversal never reach child enumeration or file reads. Lowercase encoded
  slashes and descendants still work; sibling project prefixes do not match.
- Invalid direct IDs return empty/null before local or remote I/O. Discovered
  traversal IDs are filtered. Non-UUID, embedded-dot and literal-percent IDs
  remain valid; conflicting summary IDs/cwds cannot redirect search or reads.
- Local `/var`, `/tmp`, `/etc` aliases work in both directions. SSH keeps remote
  `/private` paths distinct, supports root-project filtering and directory
  symlinks, and ignores unresolved/file links. This does not certify realpath
  containment or an actual remote OS.
- Missing/permission failures stay quiet; disconnects from directory, stat and
  file operations report context. A fake-clock case checks all 19 sessions
  complete with at most six overlapping file reads and no lost results.
- Two cases exercise the real remote-fs functions with injected fake SSH. An
  independently specified command allowlist checks every ls/find/cat/stat path,
  including apostrophes, Unicode, spaces, dollar signs, command substitution and
  semicolons. Tilde expands only on the remote. No shell command is executed.
  The remote suite asserts zero local readdir/readFile/stat or default exec calls.

Independent read-only review found no blocking issue. Its cleanup suggestions
were applied: restore real timers before assertions and check default exec calls
before clearing each error-loop iteration. No tests were executed by either
reviewer. No installed account content, credentials, provider calls or live SSH
hosts were accessed. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Native schema and implementation

Public source pin: `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8`, candidate
package 1.0.70. Newly inspected files are under
`tmp/grok-tier1-04-05-schema/`; source evidence is not binary qualification.

define current rows, human origins, ordered reasoning text and legacy upgrade
precedence. The pinned [JSONL reader](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/session/storage/jsonl/mod.rs)
accepts mixed current/legacy layouts. Supporting source hashes:

| `xai-grok-shell/src/session/storage/jsonl/mod.rs` | `477c92b5bba72df345b86dd5f1884a80654bc8ea06c9d1de101cd376a1bdb083` |
| `xai-grok-shell/src/session/usage_file.rs`        | `bdb1a2486a929dbab6f904ac2774f5551ff6e3dd490bbb7cb5633eadbb8d559a` |

Production scope is `src/main/storage/grok-session-storage.ts` and the optional
fields in `src/main/agents/session-storage.ts` (**342 changed lines**).

- Reuses `parseNativeLines`, retaining source-line provenance and explicit
  unknown/malformed notices instead of silently dropping records or throwing on
  null/scalar JSON. Source-line IDs stay stable through pagination and append.
- `includeNativeRecords` returns ordered system/user/assistant/reasoning/backend
  tool/result rows and original nonblank source-line text. Missing timestamps stay
  empty. Opaque/encrypted reasoning and unknown fields survive in source evidence;
  reported summary and content text retain their order. Nothing decrypts or runs.
- Legacy `role` rows, nested function calls, image_url blocks, reasoning_content,
  singular reasoning and raw_output are normalized with native precedence.
  Existing backend IDs are not emitted again as legacy projections. Original
  source rows remain available even when the native upgrade view deduplicates.
- Explicit human/interjection/parent_human_message/direct_bash origins stay
  visible. Default History still hides system/context/reasoning and empty rows;
  native mode retains them. Default search remains user/assistant-only.
- Calls retain native IDs and raw/parsed arguments. Results pair only with one
  preceding call bearing the same ID; duplicate, missing and out-of-order linkage
  remains explicit. Unfinished historical calls are unknown, not running tools.
- Default tool cards reuse `compactToolOutput`; native mode keeps results at
  their original positions without duplicating their output onto the call.
  Images/references from matched results are promoted only in the display view.
  `isInlineImageDataUrl` limits rendered images to embedded data; external/path
  references remain data in `imageReferences`, without reads or fetches.
- Raw rows are returned only in native mode. Legacy projections and folded tool
  cards reference their source line without repeating raw bytes. This is a context
  export input, not a whole-file byte archive or certified native migration.
  GT1-04-09 must opt in, preserve pagination/account scope and redact before export.

# GT1-04-05A native summary and lifetime usage

Completed the summary/usage continuation from [[GROK-TIER1-04]], using
[[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Production scope is two existing files:
`src/main/storage/grok-session-storage.ts` and
`src/main/agents/session-storage.ts`. No new backend, pricing implementation,
provider call, installed-account read or native mutation. Images analyzed: **0**.

Pre-task HEAD: `aaa946de247af5b2adadcb832f73f2149809d00e`. Checkout and branch
match run-state. Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` remains
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, merge base remains
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; ahead/behind is **186/10**.
Neither tip is an ancestor of the other. No branch/ancestry changes.

## Implemented behavior

- Missing, empty, malformed and non-object summaries no longer hide a usable
  transcript. Native metadata reports summary availability and retains the parsed
  object, including optional parent/fork fields, `chat_format_version` and unknown
  extensions. These are opaque evidence, never trusted read paths or instructions.
  Native title precedence is trimmed nonempty `generated_title`, then
  `session_summary`. Invalid/absent dates remain empty; no file mtime becomes a
  creation or modification date. Unknown dates sort after dated sessions.
- `usage.json.session` is normalized once. Neither `turns` nor completed children
  are added again. Cache reads and creation are subtracted from inclusive input
  exactly once; reasoning remains an output subset and is retained at session and
  model level. Missing/invalid/unsafe counts remain absent in accounting detail;
  required legacy numeric fields retain their zero fallback with explicit unknown
  or partial accounting. Missing legacy usage has unknown cost and completeness.
- `preserveJsonNumberTokens` precedes BOM-tolerant JSON parsing. Canonical
  nonnegative ticks through signed-i64 maximum `9223372036854775807` stay strings
  in `UsageCost`; `usageCostUsd` supplies only the display approximation. Negative,
  fractional, exponent and out-of-range ticks cannot become known cost. Flags
  preserve partial/incomplete observations and taint model projections. No rates
  or costs are invented. Explicit stored zero is retained, consistent with phase
  03; absence remains unknown. Upstream filters REST wire zero before persistence,
  so a missing tick field is never interpreted as a free call.
- Optional `AgentSessionInfo.accounting` reuses `UsageAccounting`: a native-session
  snapshot of the CLI runtime ledger, with `runtime-stream` provenance and
  `native-history` route, not provider billing authority. Completed children are
  already included; still-running child completeness remains unknown. Validated
  directory identity wins over an inherited `usage.sessionId` on a fork.
- Raw model IDs, optional token totals and structured costs survive in
  `accounting.models`. The numeric-only legacy `byModel` projection is emitted
  only when every model row is complete with exact cost; no incomplete subset or
  fabricated zero cost is published. Optional `reasoningTokens` preserves known
  lifetime reasoning even when no model split exists.
- Removed obsolete local summary/usage interfaces, numeric tick conversion and
  zero-coercing helper. No shared cache-version bump is required for these optional
  Grok-only fields: Grok does not use `SessionInfoCache`. Accounting consumers and
  their caches remain GT1-04-11, with explicit unknown-cost handling still required.

Pinned public-source receipts are the unchanged files listed in GT1-04-05 above
at commit `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` (candidate package 1.0.70).
Additionally inspected `xai-chat-state/src/usage.rs` establishes completed-child
folding and the distinction between ledger and running-child completeness.
These are source receipts, not exact-binary qualification.

# GT1-04-05B bounded native reads and pagination

Completed the bounded-read continuation in [[GROK-TIER1-04]], using [[CONTRACT]],
[[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD:
`219348e2d5177f11deb90e49f1febad9e05cccde`. Root and branch match run-state.
Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base is
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **187/10**.
Neither tip is an ancestor of the other. No ancestry change, native account
read, provider launch, paid call or historical tool execution. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and bounds

Three production files changed: `src/main/storage/grok-session-storage.ts`,
`src/main/utils/remote-fs.ts`, and `src/main/agents/session-storage.ts`.
Guide/source searches found no reusable bounded text-file reader; existing
provider stat caps did not bound subsequent growth. Existing account resolution,
POSIX joins, remote escaping/admission/retries/concurrency and shared pagination
remain the owners of those behaviors.

- Transcript budget is **64 MiB**, below the existing 100 MiB SSH exec ceiling.
  Summary and usage budgets are **1 MiB each**, including optional schema/fork
  metadata already embedded in the pinned summary schema. No speculative
  standalone metadata file or second storage backend was introduced.
- Local reads stat before opening, check the open descriptor, reject nonregular
  files, and read at most budget plus one sentinel byte in 64 KiB chunks. The
  descriptor closes in `finally`. Nonblocking open avoids a FIFO replacement
  hanging the reader. A post-read descriptor stat checks growth before combining
  chunks; size/mtime/initial inode and decoded byte-count disagreement marks the
  observation partial. Rejected files never reach JSON parsing.
- `readFileRemote(path, config, undefined, { maxBytes })` is an opt-in extension;
  unbounded existing callers retain their command and result behavior. An escaped
  regular-file check and `head -c <budget+1>` with stdin redirection bound remote
  transfer during growth. Invalid budgets throw; overflow and exec max-buffer
  errors return `errorCode: 'too-large'`. Grok stats before and after, checks the
  returned byte count, and discloses missing post-read stats or file changes.
- Physical source lines are capped at **100,000** before `parseNativeLines` can
  allocate its split array. The same ceiling bounds combined rows/tool calls/
  legacy projections before normalization creates additional objects. These
  sources are rejected wholly, so accepted line IDs never shift through slicing.
  Malformed/truncated JSONL rows still survive native export and mark the source
  partial. Unknown rows retain the existing explicit evidence behavior.
- Optional `SessionMessagesResult.source` distinguishes available, partial and
  unavailable transcript observations independently of pagination. Missing scope,
  oversize/line/projection limits and unreadable sources cannot masquerade as
  complete empty transcripts. `nativeMetadata.sources` records summary, usage and
  transcript observations for listed sessions. An available source describes one
  file read, not a consistent cross-file snapshot or certified native migration.
- RangeError/oversize and environmental filesystem failures stay local; unexpected
  faults still reach `captureException`. Existing remote missing/permission
  classification is retained; unexpected SSH disconnects remain reportable.
  Invalid sidecars are explicit, and changed/unavailable usage never supplies an
  exact ledger projection or fabricated known cost.
- Finite pagination values are truncated and clamped: limits **1-1000**, offset
  **0-total messages**. Nonfinite/missing limits use existing defaults (100 list,
  20 messages); nonfinite/missing offsets use zero. Shared cursor/end-relative
  pagination remains authoritative. Account and host selection are unchanged.
- Removed the now-unused stat-only storage method and unrestricted local
  `fs.readFile` path. No unused helper remains from this refactor.

## Authored coverage and static finding

- Exact 64 MiB transcript and 1 MiB summary/usage caps, including multibyte text,
  and oversized sidecars rejected before opening. Listed history survives an
  unavailable ledger with unknown cost and explicit per-source provenance.
- Growth after stat/open, one-byte sentinel overflow, growth after the final
  read, special-file rejection before/after opening, and descriptor cleanup on
  every success/failure. Five stages cover RangeError, six expected filesystem
  errors and reportable EMFILE, with source availability and Sentry assertions.
- Local and remote empty sources differ from unreadable sources. Remote missing
  post-read stats preserve partial provenance; disconnects remain reportable.
  The adapter asserts bounded options and rejects injected oversized output,
  helper overflow and post-stat growth. Existing remote teardown proves no local
  filesystem access or real SSH execution.
- Invalid UTF-8, valid U+FFFD, and a valid multibyte sequence spanning 64 KiB read
  chunks. Nested raw/tool arrays cover exact and excessive projection budgets,
  including aggregate amplification across rows. Pagination covers defaults,
  nonfinite/negative/fractional limits and the 1000-item ceiling with 1001 entries,
  cursor continuity and stable end-relative message IDs. Existing offset-clamp
  cases remain in place. List pagination reuses one normalized fixture session
  with an injected 1001-item list to avoid retaining hundreds of MiB of mock
  read buffers per case; message reads still parse all 1001 native rows.

Independent source review confirmed a **production defect**, not a test-run
failure: malformed bytes `F0 90 80` before a JSON quote decode to one U+FFFD.
Both representations occupy three bytes, so the existing decoded-length check
misses corruption when metadata is stable. The new local regression requires
partial provenance and is expected to fail until repaired. The remote helper
also decodes stdout before storage sees it, so local validation alone cannot
certify remote encoding. Checking for U+FFFD would reject legitimate text;
validating chunks independently would reject valid split sequences.

Added the bounded **GT1-04-06E1 IMPLEMENT** checkbox before GT1-04-06C to repair

# GT1-04-06E1 native UTF8 provenance repair

Repaired raw-byte validation in the existing Grok storage reader and bounded
`readFileRemote` path for [[GROK-TIER1-04]]. Read [[CONTRACT]], run-state,
phase 01 schema evidence, phase 02 account rules and phase 03 native identity
ownership. Their qualification limits remain unchanged. Images analyzed: **0**.

Pre-task HEAD: `15d9712832f52c9e6cfb893f559aac5a87248e7d`. Execution root and
branch match run-state. Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` remains
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, merge base is
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **191/10**.
Neither tip is an ancestor of the other. No branch/primary checkout changes;
unrelated `.sloptimize/` artifacts remain excluded.

## Implementation and boundaries

- Guide/source searches found no canonical general byte-validation helper.
  Reused Node's `isUtf8` from `node:buffer`, supported by the repository's
  Node >=22 requirement, without another decoder or utility implementation.
- Local bounded chunks are concatenated and validated before lossy decoding.
  Malformed bytes, including equal-length `F0 90 80`, retain readable records
  with partial `changed-during-read` provenance, matching GT1-04-06E's authored
  controls. Legitimate U+FFFD and multibyte characters across chunks remain valid.
  Original byte size, sentinel limits and descriptor `finally` cleanup remain.
- Bounded remote reads use the existing escaped `head` command through the same
  admitted, timed, retried and concurrency-limited SSH machinery. As with the
  existing remote binary reader, base64 preserves bytes over the text-only exec
  channel. The envelope includes a newline plus `head`'s exit status, so a
  successful base64 pipeline cannot conceal a failed upstream read.
- The receiver bounds the encoded length before allocation, accepts GNU/BSD
  line wrapping, validates base64 and the short status trailer, then checks the
  original-byte cap and UTF-8 before decoding. Invalid UTF-8 returns
  `errorCode: 'invalid-encoding'`; Grok exposes unavailable provenance without
  reporting expected corrupt input as an unexpected Sentry fault. Missing or
  malformed envelopes remain explicit failures. Existing upstream read errors
  retain their missing/permission mapping.
- Grok's 64 MiB transcript and 1 MiB metadata caps, plus sentinel and framing,
  fit the 100 MiB SSH output ceiling after base64 expansion. Generic budgets
  nearer 100 MiB can hit the transport ceiling sooner and return `too-large`;
  no successful truncated read is returned. Unbounded callers keep their
  existing command and return behavior. Selected account/host scope is unchanged.
- Independent static review found no blocking issue. Production scope is two
  existing files; no native provider files or historical tools were accessed.
  This repairs corruption disclosure only, not cross-file snapshot consistency
  (GT1-04-06A/06B) or native migration qualification.

It should include malformed base64/trailers, read failure after partial output,

chunk boundaries, and overflow sentinel splits. GT1-04-13 executes all suites.

## Writer evidence and implementation

The pinned public source `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` receipts
under `tmp/grok-tier1-04-05-schema/` retain the hashes recorded in GT1-04-05/05A.
Independent source inspection found:

- `xai-grok-shell/src/session/persistence.rs:1-14` describes a FIFO actor and
  per-file atomic rename, without a shared multi-file commit marker.
- `storage/jsonl/mod.rs:1333-1351` appends transcript before updating summary
  count; `1803-1824` similarly rewrites transcript before updating the count.
  Append tails may be torn. Provider append locks do not cover full rewrites.
- `persistence.rs:2596` and `usage_file.rs:10-23` persist lifetime usage separately.
  Usage timestamps, summary counts, attach attempts and cwd generations cannot
  bind the three files to one completed transaction. Fork usage may retain a
  parent's session ID, so the selected directory remains the identity authority.
- `xai-chat-state/src/usage.rs:6` establishes that completed child totals are
  already folded once. Persisted files contain no inventory proving whether
  other children still run. No child files or turn detail are summed again.

Guide/source searches inspected `SessionInfoCache.fileFingerprint`, shared
atomic writers and native translation readers. No existing helper supplies a
multi-file native snapshot; the cache fingerprint assumes append-only files and
cannot establish Grok rewrite consistency. The implementation stays in the
existing Grok adapter, with no new storage backend, cache or interface.

`loadSessionInfo` samples summary, transcript and usage, then performs exactly
one bounded comparison read of each. Local comparison includes device, inode,
size, mtime and ctime; content comparison also catches equal-size remote rewrites
within SSH stat's one-second timestamp resolution. Missing or changed samples
produce `changed-or-unavailable-during-snapshot`. Matching samples still report
`cross-file-generation-unverified`, because the provider has no common epoch.
Every otherwise available source in the combined result becomes partial;
stronger individual corruption/unavailability reasons remain intact. This is
explicit uncertainty, not a claimed transactional snapshot or writer-idle proof.

Complete usage/model accounting becomes partial in the combined result. Exact
tick strings and raw per-model buckets survive; unknown cost stays unknown.
The legacy `byModel` projection is withheld because it cannot express partial
cost. Reported numeric totals remain display projections of the observed ledger.
`parseGrokUsageFile` alone retains its isolated-ledger semantics. Completed-child
inclusion remains `completed`, running-child completeness remains `unknown`.

Local bounded reads now re-stat the pathname after the descriptor read, catching
atomic replacement invisible to descriptor stats alone. Post-read growth beyond
the cap and RangeError reject as too-large; environmental loss preserves partial
provenance, unexpected faults retain Sentry reporting, and descriptors close in
all paths. Remote transcript-only message/search reads reuse one bounded content
comparison. A list-provided transcript does not trigger a third sample.

All reads remain scoped to the selected account directory and SSH configuration,
using existing escaped remote helpers and concurrency limits. No polling,
provider locks, writes, real account reads, provider calls or credentials are
used. Byte/line/parser caps and source-line IDs are retained. Independent static
review passed after adding the remote transcript recheck and final-path cap/
RangeError handling. Source scope is one production file, below 500 changed lines.

Completed the bounded desktop resume-safety portion of [[GROK-TIER1-04]],
following [[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]], [[03]] and
[[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]]. The full account/host/history/runtime work exceeds

`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base:
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; ahead/behind **196/10**.
No ancestry or primary-checkout changes. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and reuse

- `useAgentSessionIdListener.ts` accepts Grok IDs only for an explicitly named,
  existing AI tab. Missing/closed targets and blank IDs cannot borrow the active
  tab or write a session-level fallback. Confirmed IDs use the existing
  `resolveTurnProvider` / `updateProviderSlot` ownership helpers and remain
  available even when an allocated turn was cancelled. No ID event means no
  manufactured ID. The legacy origin bridge writes Claude's index, so Grok no
  longer calls it.
- A different returned Grok ID retains the previous native ID, transcript,
  usage and context estimate. The normal error fields/log and a toast disclose
  the refusal. A parked Grok slot remains parked; its mismatch does not pause
  the newly selected provider. Other-provider ID behavior is unchanged.
- `useAgentErrorListener.ts` keeps Grok `session_not_found` as a blocking
  provider error, preserving identity and history without a fresh-session
  recovery action. It uses the error's provider, then the originating tab's
  captured provider for legacy events. A late Grok failure after a provider
  switch appends evidence through `updateAiTab`, after flushing buffered output,
  and returns before retries, current-provider errors, Auto Run pauses or modal
  side effects. Existing group-chat recovery is unchanged.

Read the indexed provider/process/state/platform/IPC guidance and existing
listener/test patterns before editing. No utility, type, IPC or history backend
was added. Independent static review found and resolved provider-switch
misclassification, stale-error side effects and name normalization regressions.

# GT1-04-07A durable native session binding

Implemented the tab/parking portion of [[GROK-TIER1-04]] using [[CONTRACT]],
[[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD:
`984293e16509db2becd7a69d4657b36837db0e64`. Root/branch match run-state;
base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **198/10**.
No branch/base changes. Unrelated `.sloptimize/` remains excluded.
Images analyzed: **0**.

## Persistence contract and reuse

Three production files, **106 changed lines**, extend existing ownership paths:

- `agents/providerSwap.ts` defines `NativeSessionBinding`. Version 1 is either
  `state: 'unknown'` with `reason: 'legacy' | 'unobserved'`, or `state: 'bound'`
  with `provider`, `nativeSessionId`, `accountRef`, `host: { kind, identity }`
  and `cwd`. Every field is readonly, including host fields. Bindings are
  replaced atomically because parking/closed snapshots can share references.
  Missing legacy fields mean unknown, never ownership inferred from matching
  IDs or current configuration. Null native IDs have no resumable binding.
- `accountRef` uses `resolveAgentAccountRef` on the actual canonical provider
  home on the executing host. Remote defaults/tilde must first be resolved on
  that host; the local home cannot stand in. It is storage ownership, not
  authenticated billing identity. `host.identity` is a stable observed executing
  machine/endpoint reference; it must not be an SSH config ID/alias or literal
  local marker. Managed consumers use the qualified endpoint installation
  identity, excluding runtime/process epochs. No credentials or environment
  bundles belong in the value. It is provenance, not managed authorization.
- `ProviderTabSession`, `ProviderSwitchTab` and renderer `AITab` carry the same
  type. `updateProviderSlot` preserves usage-only updates and handles live and
  parked slots consistently. Replacing a scoped native ID without observed
  replacement ownership marks it unknown; null resets clear it. Undefined IDs
  in partial updates are omitted. Explicit bound updates must agree with the
  owning provider and resulting native ID, or fail before changing the tab.
- `translation/session.ts` carries scope with outgoing/incoming provider slots
  and its saved inventory. New destination mappings are unknown even when an ID
  equals a previous destination ID. Only replay of the same committed operation
  may retain scope observed since that commit. Source ownership remains parked.
  This does not register or enable Grok translation.

Read existing account/ACP/translation/managed identity types before defining the
contract. Translation identities lack executing-host ownership; managed endpoint
references include process epochs, so neither is a durable resume binding as-is.
Independent review found matching-ID translation reuse and explicit-undefined ID
hazards; both were repaired before final checks. No new utility or history backend.

Active/snoozed persistence and reload already spread full tab records in
`useDebouncedPersistence.ts` and `useSessionRestoration.ts`. Closed desktop
snapshots in `utils/tabHelpers/index.ts` and runtime `agents/closed-tabs.ts`
retain the field via full-record copies; store reads preserve unknown fields.
Desktop undo stacks intentionally do not survive app restart; this packet does
not change that policy. The runtime closed-tab archive remains durable.

## Exact consumer and test handoff

- GT1-04-07A1 finishes typed `HistoryEntry` / `AITabRecord` declarations in two
  existing files. This is the only remaining persistence declaration work split
  by the three-file bound. Runtime records already preserve unknown fields.
- GT1-04-07B/07C must resolve selected history owner/account/host and compare the
  full binding. `tabHelpers/index.ts` closed restore currently deduplicates by
  native ID alone (legacy/unified restore paths); 07C must include that consumer
  before claiming same-ID account/host isolation, splitting under CONTRACT if
  its three-file budget is exceeded. Never relabel a closed snapshot's provider.
- GT1-04-07D supplies actual canonical account, executing endpoint and cwd from
  immutable admitted launch context. Unknown ownership must refuse required
  scoped resume; it cannot become bound from a matching ID. Process generation
  stays an event/admission fence, separate from durable host identity.
- GT1-04-07E persists the observed native ID and binding together in the owning
  slot after rejecting stale generation/tab ownership. GT1-04-07F applies the
  same binding to runtime slots and History from the original turn context.
  These consumers still own validation of untrusted persisted shapes, nonblank
  scope, account/host/cwd comparison, reset paths and launch admission. An
  optional TypeScript field alone enforces none of those boundaries.
- GT1-04-07AT authors providerSwap, translation-session and debounced-persistence
  cases. GT1-04-08 retains process/IPC/recording integration ownership;
  GT1-04-13 executes all affected suites. No tests were run or collected here.

## Implementation and consumer handoff

Three production files, **243 changed lines**, extend the existing APIs:

- `src/main/ipc/handlers/agentSessions.ts` resolves a live owner, rejects a
  provider mismatch, and captures its account selection through the phase 02
  `resolveAgentAccountDir` wrapper with the captured owner. Explicit missing,
  blank or invalid owners cannot fall through to the default account.
- The existing `listPaginated` owner option now validates the same scope as
  `read`, `list`, `search` and `getPath`. `read` accepts `ownerAgentId` within
  its options. The other three methods accept an optional final `ownerAgentId`
  after the existing `sshRemoteId`; positional legacy arguments are unchanged.
- `resolveSshLaunchTarget` and the canonical store getter reject unavailable,
  disabled, unselected and mismatched SSH targets before native reads or mirror
  access. Saved malformed SSH configuration is refused, including false/zero/
  empty-string values identified and repaired during independent review.
  Current configured SSH selection wins; legacy observed `sshRemoteId` is used
  only when no configuration exists. Enabled remote owners cannot read locally.
- Account resolution preserves blank-unset precedence and selected-root absence.
  Remote defaults remain remote `~/.grok`; no local home is used to expand them.
  The existing explicit `read.configDir` remains supported and is checked for
  type/NUL errors; explicit blanks retain phase 02's provider-default semantics.
  Combining an owner with a raw account override is rejected rather than
  silently replacing the owner's account.
- Grok history reads never invoke transcript restoration: the mirror has no
  account/host identity and cannot reconstruct Grok's multi-file native state.
  Scoped reads for other providers also bypass the unscoped mirror; ordinary
  legacy local reads retain their prior restoration behavior.
- `src/main/preload/sessions.ts` forwards the scope without changing unscoped
  argument shapes. `src/renderer/global.d.ts` exposes the owner contracts and
  reuses canonical `SessionReadOptions` / `SessionMessagesResult`, preserving
  pagination, native-record options, and partial/unavailable source provenance.

GT1-04-07C must pass the chosen owner on every history read and recheck selection
following asynchronous work. This API resolves the current selection; it does
not certify immutable native ownership or create `NativeSessionBinding` values.
Observed account/endpoint capture, retained-scope admission, and persistence
remain GT1-04-07D/07E/07F. No native files are mutated by this implementation.

GT1-04-07BT owns focused tests in the existing IPC and preload suites. Cover
colliding IDs across accounts/hosts, missing/provider-mismatched owners, blank
account configuration, absent selected roots, remote custom/default homes,
missing/disabled/unselected/conflicting remotes, malformed saved SSH settings,
owner-plus-account override refusal, all five routes, no mirror access on
rejection/Grok reads, native source provenance and unchanged pagination/legacy
calls. Replace existing expectations that missing remotes read local Claude
history: this unsafe fallback is deliberately removed for every provider.
GT1-04-08 retains process/resume integration; GT1-04-13 executes both boundary
suites along with its existing suite inventory.

## Implemented boundary and explicit limitation

Three production files, **242 changed lines**, reuse `canonicalJson`,
`resolveSessionProjectPath`, `createTab` and `updateProviderSlot`:

- `resumableSession.ts` accepts the chosen Maestro owner and canonical retained
  native binding. Grok reads forward `ownerAgentId` even when display logs were
  supplied. Unknown/malformed/mismatched bindings and missing owners are refused;
  incomplete, unavailable or wrong-provider source provenance cannot replace
  retained content. Complete bindings compare account, endpoint, cwd, provider
  and native ID, with no new account resolver or history parser.
- **Grok history reopening is intentionally unavailable in this intermediate
  packet.** GT1-04-07B's read result exposes availability but no observed native
  ownership receipt. A selected owner, matching ID or retained logs cannot prove
  the canonical account and actual executing endpoint. After a scoped read the
  service explicitly refuses until GT1-04-07C1 supplies and validates that typed
  receipt. It never reaches the unscoped Grok origins/name/star/context lookup.
  No speculative IPC arguments, type assertions or fabricated bindings were added.
- `useAgentSessionManagement.ts` forwards the Grok owner and selected binding,
  compares full scope for tab matching and persists supplied valid scope through
  the canonical provider-slot updater. Its in-memory selection key includes
  owner/provider, cwd/project/remote paths, both SSH selectors, executable/args
  and explicit env. It is never logged or persisted. Fresh store checks reject
  changed or removed owners, changed/rebound/closed selected tabs and superseded
  requests. A no-op update returns false; success follows an actual committed
  focus/create. Existing logs/usage/context survive refusal. The dependency now
  explicitly requires the synchronous Zustand store action.
- `quickChatEngine.ts` refuses unscoped closed Grok candidates and remembered
  Grok homes before ID-only reopening. Expected refusal marks the turn failed
  and remains latched across follow-up sends; only an explicit new/tab choice
  or new chat resets it. Recall reports the refusal while preserving remembered
  messages, without an ignored rejecting `.then` promise. Selection is checked
  after routing and reopen, so a provider switch cannot fall through to a fresh
  conversation. Existing non-Grok fallback behavior remains for unchanged scope.

These are caller safeguards, not native-resume certification. No provider was
launched, no account/conversation content was read, and no SSH or paid call ran.
Unknown legacy ownership stays unknown; Grok remains beta. Static review found
and repaired a stale Quick Chat provider check and clarified the synchronous
setter contract. Final narrow review found no further blocking regression.

## Contract and validation

Three production files reuse `NativeSessionBinding`, `resolveAgentAccountDir`,
`resolveAgentAccountRef`, `installationIdentity`, `canonicalJson` and existing
transcript conversion. No second storage/parser or host identity scheme:

- Read options and results now carry the canonical binding. A requested binding
  cannot label a result: the adapter must validate its actual native source
  before returning a receipt. Missing/unknown ownership remains distinct from
  partial/unavailable content. Existing preload/global types inherit the fields.
- The IPC handler rejects unknown or provider/native-ID-mismatched retained scope
  before storage. For owned local Grok reads it resolves physical account/cwd,
  matches the owner's project, uses the existing desktop installation identity
  without process epochs, and compares the full retained binding before content
  I/O. It passes canonical roots and an isolated scope copy to the adapter.
- Immutable private selection keys capture owner/provider/project/cwd, explicit
  environment/launch overrides, effective account and cloned SSH definition.
  They are never logged or persisted. Selection and canonical paths are checked
  before/after asynchronous reads; changed owners, roots or receipts refuse.
  Missing roots produce unavailable/unobserved scope. No mirror is restored.
- The renderer requires Grok's retained binding and owner, forwards the binding
  even with supplied display messages, and accepts only a complete source with a
  matching receipt. It never asks the unscoped origins store for Grok names,
  stars, origin or context. Selected-record metadata can survive verified reads.
  Non-Grok legacy argument shapes and metadata behavior remain unchanged.

## Explicit incomplete wiring and bounded ownership

**Grok reopening remains unavailable in this intermediate packet.** The current
adapter does not return a verified receipt, so local reads remain unobserved;
scoped SSH reads return unavailable before native storage. No alias, unresolved
remote home, selected setting or matching ID is asserted as observed ownership.
No provider/account contents, credentials or real SSH endpoints were accessed.

Inspection found two additional source boundaries beyond this task's three-file
limit. Four unchecked implementation/test continuations were inserted immediately
before 07C1T, with concrete ownership and no certification claim:

- **07C1S / 07C1ST:** Grok's `findSessionDir` currently chooses the first matching
  ID across exact and descendant cwd folders. `getSessionPath` is only a guess.
  The actual source must be pinned and checked before content I/O, including
  canonical account/cwd and changed symlink/subtree targets. The storage adapter
  and its existing fixture suite own that work. Echoing a supplied binding alone
  would be false evidence and is expressly forbidden.
- **07C1R / 07C1RT:** history lacks the executing SSH endpoint observation port.
  Bootstrap already exposes EnterpriseService.remote; wire its qualified
  installation identity and bounded, admitted remote path resolution. The
  existing generic worktree resolver is escaped but its default executor has no
  explicit timeout/access gate. Unobserved unmanaged endpoints must remain
  unavailable until real observation exists; native SSH proof remains not-run.
- **07C1T / 07CT** retain IPC/preload and renderer coverage. Adapt old Grok owned
  read assertions to canonical local roots/source receipts and unavailable
  unobserved SSH scope. Preserve 07BT's non-Grok selection tests. **07C2-07C5**
  retain caller propagation and **07D-07F** retain launch/event/History wiring.
  **07C4** first obtains a scoped observed browser receipt; old ID-only records
  and supplied messages cannot bootstrap ownership through the resume service.

These checks establish read provenance only, never permission or launch
admission. All 134 acceptance rows and applicability remain unchanged; this
packet adds an evidence catalog entry with remaining owners. Grok stays beta.

item. GT1-04-13 owns execution after the bounded continuations.

# GT1-04-07C1S local native-source observation

Implemented the local source-observation portion of [[GROK-TIER1-04]], using
[[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD: `d383460e4f7353873f3477ef544dd1a9f2292025`.

`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base: `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; ahead/behind: **205/10**.
Unrelated `.sloptimize/` files remain excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Source ownership and read behavior

Only `src/main/storage/grok-session-storage.ts` changed in production
(**267 changed lines**). Reuses `resolveAgentAccountDir`,
`resolveAgentAccountRef`, `NativeSessionBinding`, the existing bounded descriptor
reader, transcript parser, pagination, and expected-error classification.
Existing filesystem/path helpers were inspected; they do not observe the opened
transcript's identity or establish its native cwd/ID.

- Scoped reads require a valid host-observed Grok binding and an explicit
  selected account (including an instance-bound account). They compare its
  canonical account reference and cwd with physical directory observations.
  Storage consumes the caller's host identity; it creates no identity scheme.
- Candidate selection uses exact canonical cwd. A descendant's colliding ID
  cannot supply the requested session. Multiple logical cwd aliases containing
  that ID are ambiguous, even when they target the same physical directory.
- Account-root aliases resolve to the canonical account. Selected sessions-pool
  aliases are observed independently, including shared pools outside that account,
  without relabeling the selected account. Physical cwd-folder/session ancestry
  must remain inside the selected pool, and the physical session basename must
  match the requested native ID. Transcript aliases must stay inside that session.
- Directory/file device and inode observations are checked before and after
  asynchronous reads. The bounded reader opens the physical path with
  `O_NOFOLLOW`, checks the descriptor before any content read, and rechecks
  candidate uniqueness and pinned paths. Replaced paths discard all content and
  the receipt; no candidate/account fallback runs after refusal.
- Ownership remains independent of content completeness. A stable observed
  source can carry a receipt with oversized/unavailable or malformed/partial
  content. Same-inode appends retain existing partial-source reporting. Historical
  tools stay inert, provider files remain untouched, and legacy unscoped browsing
  retains its prior behavior and returns no native binding.
- Missing, non-directory, inaccessible, cyclic or ambiguous sources refuse.
  Device/inode values that cannot be represented safely also remain unobserved.
  An inaccessible or malformed unrelated encoded cwd can conservatively refuse
  the scoped read because uniqueness could not be established; it does not
  justify selecting a different candidate. Deferred tests must cover this limit.

## Bounded continuation and test ownership

The current remote helper exposes only size/mtime/directory status, not physical
source or opened-descriptor identity. Scoped SSH reads therefore explicitly
return `native-source-unobserved` without local filesystem fallback or a fabricated
receipt. Existing unscoped remote browsing is unchanged.

CONTRACT's bounded-packet rule splits the additional remote helper/adapter work
into **GT1-04-07C1SR**, with helper/storage tests in **GT1-04-07C1SRT**. Both
unchecked continuations precede dependent coverage in the canonical playbook.
**GT1-04-07C1ST** retains local source/alias/race tests, **07C1R/07C1RT** retain
executing-endpoint qualification, and **GT1-04-13** executes the suites. This is
source-read provenance, not native resume/launch qualification. Later caller and
launch propagation tasks remain open. Grok remains beta; all 134 acceptance rows
and applicability decisions remain unchanged.

# GT1-04-07C1SR remote native-source observation

Completed the remote source-observation packet in [[GROK-TIER1-04]], following
[[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD: `93532ac42d8052fd9725a46811f7f2f03b7a4b80`.
Execution root/branch match run-state. Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base:
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; ahead/behind **206/10**.
Unrelated `.sloptimize/` remains excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and scope

Two production files, **486 changed lines**, extend the existing local ownership
verifier and remote filesystem boundary. Reused the canonical account resolver,
POSIX path operations, shell escaping, remote admission, per-host concurrency and
retry bounds, bounded base64/UTF-8 reader, native parser and pagination.

- `observePathRemote` observes canonical targets and decimal-string device/inode
  identity, preserving large inode values. It validates response shape, absolute
  normalized paths, positive inodes and bounded strings; no size/mtime pair or
  configured SSH alias substitutes for ownership.
- Guarded `readFileRemote` opens the canonical file with nonblocking/no-follow
  flags, compares the actual descriptor and selected alias observations before
  any content read, and checks them again afterward. Its receipt is emitted only
  after these checks; changed targets discard content and ownership. Descriptor
  size/mtime/ctime changes mark content partial independently of ownership.
- The adapter uses exact canonical cwd, selected account and unique native ID.
  Same-ID descendants cannot supply content; aliases with duplicate IDs refuse.
  Non-directory/dangling same-ID entries participate in ambiguity checks. Account
  and sessions-subtree aliases retain the selected account reference, with
  physical ancestry and logical/physical encoded cwd checks around reads.
- Successful descriptor proof can accompany rejected oversized/invalid-UTF-8
  content; missing proof never carries a binding. Historical tools remain inert.
  Legacy unbound local/remote browsing and unguarded helper commands are unchanged.
  No selected remote path is opened through the local filesystem.
- Remote observations require POSIX Python 3 with descriptor APIs. The isolated
  `python3 -I -S -B` invocation avoids project/user imports and bytecode cache
  writes. Missing Python or unsupported identity is explicitly unavailable.
  The observer has a **25-second alarm**, inside the existing **30-second SSH
  command limit** and finite retry policy. Reads retain the 64 MiB transcript cap
  and one-byte sentinel, inside the existing 100 MiB exec output cap. Observation
  JSON is limited to 100,000 characters, paths to 16,384 characters and read
  guards to 16 observations. Scoped discovery refuses more than 1,000 cwd entries
  or incomplete enumeration instead of asserting uniqueness. A large or
  uninspectable unrelated cwd tree can therefore conservatively refuse the read.
- Expected missing/permission/unsupported/managed-refusal outcomes stay quiet;
  unexpected SSH and filesystem failures retain error reporting. Independent
  reviews caught and repaired swallowed unexpected errors, zero-inode acceptance
  and potential Python import cache writes before completion.

The storage adapter **consumes a caller-established endpoint identity**; it does
not observe or invent one. **07C1R/07C1RT** still own endpoint qualification and
bootstrap wiring, so this packet alone does not enable certified remote reopen.
**07C1SRT** authors fake-remote/inert-SSH coverage, including Python availability,

  reads; failed reads and post-read changes must close every descriptor and return

  UTF-8 bytes, the 65,536-byte chunk boundary, oversized pre-read files, bounded
  growth and the one-byte sentinel. Assertions bound program output separately
  from the harness's 3-second/1-MiB process ceilings. The existing suite retains
  the production default 30-second SSH timeout assertions.

  symlink and mkdir operations. Only explicit simulated-writer injections may
  mutate fixtures. Directory listings, transcript bytes and unchanged-file mtime
  are checked after reads; isolated execution disables bytecode writes.

  Descriptor-only inode/device mismatches refuse before bytes are read; handles close.

  ownership independently of partial/unavailable content, with explicit reasons.
  Legacy reads remain unbound and do not perform physical ownership observations.

Unrelated `.sloptimize/` remains excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and boundaries

Two production files, **155 changed lines**, reuse the existing qualifier,
account reference, environment resolver, canonical JSON fence and native storage
receipt contract:

- Bootstrap forwards a live getter for `EnterpriseService.remote` into History.
  Owned Grok remote reads require `qualifyResolved` to return the same SSH
  configuration and a qualified endpoint installation identity. Durable bindings
  exclude runtime/process epochs and never substitute a hostname, SSH alias or
  this desktop's installation identity.
- The existing `observePathRemote` from 07C1SR resolves requested cwd, owner cwd
  and selected account on that endpoint. All three must be directories, and cwd
  aliases must resolve to the same physical object. Canonical account/cwd paths
  reach storage; device/inode observations remain private to the read fence.
  Relative roots are explicitly unavailable; remote tilde resolution never uses
  local HOME. Unchanged paths with changed physical objects are rejected.
- Endpoint qualification brackets path observations before and after adapter I/O.
  Existing selection checks include effective configured `HOME`/`GROK_HOME`
  values from remote/global/provider/session layers, without local process env.
  The adapter must independently confirm the same source binding. Unknown or
  mismatched receipts never become bound; partial/unavailable content provenance
  remains independent of ownership. No mirror or native files are restored.
- Desktop SSH launch and canonical account selection do not yet reconcile every
  environment layer. Configured default-root `GROK_HOME`, or default/tilde-root
  `HOME`, therefore produces `history-remote-account-environment-unresolved`.
  This conservative refusal includes configured blanks; explicit absolute
  selected roots remain readable. 07D owns final launch environment/managed-pin
  reconciliation and retained-binding comparison before native resume.
- Unmanaged endpoint binding remains explicitly unavailable:
  `remoteExpectation` has no enrollment to compare, `readRemoteAttestation`
  accepts only `report.managed`, and `hostManagedStanding` exposes no standalone
  installation observation. `history-remote-endpoint-unqualified` reports that
  gap without probing a default account. Unrelated local/legacy reads remain on
  their existing paths. Endpoint/path loss has a named unavailable outcome;
  unexpected observer failures propagate through IPC reporting.

The helper added after this task was planned already provides escaped physical
paths and remote-access admission, a **25-second Python alarm**, **30-second SSH
command timeout**, finite retries, **100,000-character response acceptance** and
**16,384-character path limits**, under the shared executor's **100 MiB capture
ceiling**. Those are distinct limits, not a 100 KB allocation guarantee. Reusing
it avoids the unbounded/ungated default `resolveWorktreePathsRemote` entirely;
`remote-git.ts` is unchanged. Remote observations require the existing isolated
POSIX Python 3 observer. No real SSH, provider accounts, credentials or paid turns
were used. These receipts establish read provenance, not launch authorization,
live remote certification, or completed Grok reopen UI wiring.

provenance cases to **six typed cases** for [[GROK-TIER1-04]], following

## Authored coverage and reuse

Only the two assigned test files change. The existing session factory, store
mocks, account resolver, installation identity and canonical binding types are
reused; there is no new parser, production helper or provider behavior change.

- Preserve the five-API 07BT selection matrix. Its owned-read branch now supplies
  inert physical paths and a typed qualified endpoint, expects canonical adapter
  options and unknown receipts, and treats ambiguous remote defaults as unavailable.
  Unowned explicit-root reads preserve unavailable content and legacy ownership.
- Local/SSH receipt cases require independently seeded native-source receipts,
  canonical alias/tilde account and cwd roots, exact pagination/native-record
  forwarding, stable local installation identity and an isolated binding copy.
  Same native IDs with different provider, account, cwd, host kind or installation
  are refused before content reads when retained, and after reads when returned.
  Missing fields, extra fields, malformed/unknown retained bindings also refuse.
- Available/partial/unavailable source provenance stays independent of ownership.
  Missing/unknown adapter receipts cannot be promoted from the requested binding.
  Owner removal, provider/cwd/environment/settings changes, physical replacement
  and installation changes while reading reject stale results. Remote replacement
  preserves canonical paths while changing only inode evidence. Owner changes
  after initial path observation refuse before storage; adapter mutation cannot
  rewrite the host's captured scope.
- Missing physical roots, unqualified/changing remote installations, relative
  account roots, non-directory paths and another owner cwd never reach storage.
  Every new handler case forbids transcript mirror operations. Remote cases throw
  if local realpath is used, and local cases forbid remote observers/qualifiers.
- Preload covers local/SSH available/partial/unavailable receipts with typed owner,
  retained binding, pagination and native-record options. Response identity and
  absent receipts survive forwarding; owned requests do not override configDir.

No real provider accounts, files, SSH endpoints, credentials or paid turns were
used. These are authored fixtures, not native compliance or resume certification.
The request's explicit no-test instruction overrides the generic test-execution
requirement. Tests were **not run or collected**; **GT1-04-13** owns execution.

# GT1-04-07C2 Quick Chat native ownership

Implemented the three-file Quick Chat ownership packet from [[GROK-TIER1-04]],
using [[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD:
`57e4a1d10bff0fce9e1499b2223042b12c2d7000`. The execution root and branch match run-state. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` remains
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **214/10**.
Unrelated `.sloptimize/` files remain excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and scope

- Closed History candidates retain the recorded Maestro owner and canonical
  native binding. Summaries and route keys include owner/account/host/cwd/provider
  identity; live and orphan tabs deduplicate only matching scoped conversations.
  The bounded cache also checks the existing in-memory selection fence, without
  persisting or logging its environment values. Unknown Grok history stays a
  refusal candidate rather than being silently relabelled or filtered into a new chat.
- Reopening passes the recorded owner and binding to `loadResumableSession`.
  Its existing scoped native read validates account/endpoint/cwd receipts. The
  router rechecks the captured selection and existing tab ownership inside the
  fresh store updater, including a non-Grok-to-Grok switch during the read.
  It preserves existing logs/usage and stores newly verified bindings through
  `updateProviderSlot`. Scoped read/empty/orphan/commit failures remain refusals.
- Recent chats retain per-turn and home bindings through the existing wire/store
  parser. Malformed supplied scope becomes explicit unknown; a legacy absent
  field stays absent. Remembered turns cannot acquire another tab's native ID,
  binding or reply after a selection/rebinding change. Failed recall preserves
  the remembered home scope and transcript, including subsequent saves.
- Engine fences cover delayed routing, tab disappearance/rebinding and delivery.
  Repeated queued follow-ups retain the refusal latch; explicit new/tab choice
  recovers, including selecting the current tab. Cancelled routing cannot create
  a tab after its read. Recall handles refusal on its awaited home promise and
  verifies even an already-open scoped home. A new-tab callback distinguishes a
  committed reopened tab from a concurrent or already-open tab, so recall cannot
  claim or hide another chat's existing tab.

No account resolver, native history parser or backend was added. The shared
`HistoryEntry` type describes the existing unprojected History IPC result;
`global.d.ts` still has an older inline return declaration without this optional
binding. No cast fabricates a binding. This task supplies the consumer; actual
launch/event/History ownership writers remain 07D/07E/07F. Native calls, real
account reads, SSH and paid turns: **0**. Grok remains beta; all 134 acceptance
rows and applicability are unchanged. Deterministic/live evidence is **not-run**.

  the History callback contracts rather than erasing their options/result.

  and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter. It passes recorded `sessionId` as `targetSessionId` and
  `nativeSessionBinding` unchanged, without searching by native ID or creating

# GT1-04-07C4 browser ownership and observed receipts

Implemented the three-file browser packet from [[GROK-TIER1-04]], following
[[CONTRACT]], [[01]], [[02]] and [[03]]. Pre-task HEAD:
`5c65cf6839e71d353207f178e8bd5d12257e82bd`. Execution root and branch match

Unrelated `.sloptimize/` remains excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and handoff

- The viewer forwards `ownerAgentId` on every initial/backfill transcript read,
  retains cloned canonical `NativeSessionBinding` and source provenance with the
  selected record, and sends that retained binding on subsequent pages. Existing
  `matchesNativeSessionBinding`, `resumeSelectionKey` and IPC/storage contracts
  supply validation; no new parser, account resolver or binding type was added.
- Grok pages require an observed provider/native-ID-matching binding and source.
  Missing, malformed, changed or unavailable ownership clears the selection and
  messages with an explicit notice. Partial content remains visible but cannot
  authorize resume; later available pages cannot promote earlier partial content.
- Request generations reject late pages, cleanup and focus/scroll callbacks after
  another selection, clear, scope change or unmount. Fresh store checks also catch
  owner removal or configuration changes before React rerenders. Receipt updates
  merge only source fields so a completed rename survives an outstanding read.
- A numeric generation remounts the browser contents when the private canonical
  selection changes, resetting list/search pagination, messages and selection for
  owner/provider/account-environment/SSH/cwd changes. Environment-bearing keys
  remain in memory and are never displayed, logged or persisted.
- Detailed resume forwards the selected owner and observed binding through the
  existing optional `ResumeSessionOptions` argument. Explicit conflicting scope
  refuses. Grok quick resume opens a scoped transcript read and asks the user to
  choose Resume after verification. Display messages and list rows alone do not
  authorize it. Non-Grok quick resume retains its existing path.

Global/provider settings and physical SSH endpoint changes have no renderer
change subscription here. They are checked by the main process on the next
scoped read/resume, with the retained receipt fencing the native source; this
packet does not claim immediate invalidation of already displayed text for
unobservable external changes. The existing leaf still closes eagerly after a
resume callback; await-before-close, refusal retention and standalone leaf scope
forwarding remain **GT1-04-07C5**. Its optional callback scope is preserved and
validated by the browser adapter.

**GT1-04-07CUIT/07CUIAT** own tests, including scoped initial/backfill requests,
colliding native IDs, partial-source latching, rename during pagination, stale
pages/finally/animation frames, owner removal, scope changes without render,
quick-read-first behavior, optional callback scope, and refused opens. Seed the
session store with the selected owner when exercising the browser's fresh-state
checks; keep non-Grok behavior covered. **GT1-04-13** executes the suites.

packet. No real account reads, provider execution, SSH or paid calls occurred.

# GT1-04-07C5UH1 canonical resume mutation fence

Pre-task HEAD: `e25ba0c2788bd9850ebaf6a24e325db5c405ef2d`. Execution root and branch match
[[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind
**220/10**. Neither tip is an ancestor of the other. No fetch, branch change or
primary-checkout mutation. Unrelated `.sloptimize/` is preserved and excluded.
Images analyzed: **0**.

Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/evidence/01]],
[[Plans/grok-tier1/evidence/02]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/evidence/03]]. Implementation
is **implemented** for this two-file packet; deterministic and live evidence
remain **not-run**. No acceptance row or beta/capability flag is promoted.

## Implementation and test handoff

- `ResumeSessionOptions.canResume(nextSession?)` adds optional synchronous caller
  admission. Canonical resume checks it before loading, immediately after its
  single shared loader await, within the existing synchronous session updater
  before constructing changes and again against the exact proposed result, and
  after store notification before changing `activeAgentSessionId`. Refusal before
  commit retains tabs, logs, focus, input mode and the history selector. Existing
  scoped native ownership, owner selection, tab rebind and dedup checks remain.
- Director Notes forwards its composed guard to canonical resume. It tracks
  navigation for both the recorded owner and the initially visible agent through
  `resolveActiveTabRef`/`sameTabRef`, input mode, active group and focused pane.
  Existing synchronous subscriptions latch tab/pane, agent, Group Chat, modal and
  owner-scope transitions, so returning to the original selection cannot revive
  a stale load. Ordinary log/metadata updates do not change navigation.
- The final predicate receives the exact session produced by `createTab` or
  `focusAiTabInSession`. Only observing that same object in the store advances
  the expected focus baseline. The check is centralized so session or cross-store
  notification order can recognize the accepted focus. Existing tiled tabs and
  new standalone tabs use their actual canonical transforms, with no hand-written
  focus exception, duplicate transcript loader or temporary navigation bypass.
- Synchronous subscriber navigation/removal/rebind after an admitted commit stops
  the history-selector write and outer activation/closing. The already accepted
  tab change is never rolled back over newer user state. A synchronous observer
  that clones the candidate before any guard sees its exact identity may cause a
  conservative refusal; this is safer than accepting an unobserved focus commit.
  Callers without the optional predicate continue through the same loading and
  tab transforms; the additional guard is provider independent.

**GT1-04-07CT** authors canonical regression cases: refusal before read, after a
held load, at updater entry and at final candidate admission; unchanged tabs,
logs, input mode and `activeAgentSessionId`; removed/rebound owner and unknown
required native scope; existing/new-tab success without a predicate; and a
synchronous subscriber navigation/removal before the history-selector write.
**GT1-04-07C5UT** authors composed coverage: same-owner AI/file/browser/terminal
navigation, tiled group/pane focus, away-and-back, visible-agent navigation when
the owner is different, exact new/existing/tiled resume focus success, newer
requests, caller refusal and cleanup. Use the real synchronous store and inspect
both canonical and outer mutations. **GT1-04-13** executes the suites.
Tests were intentionally **not authored, run or collected** in this IMPLEMENT
packet. No real account reads, SSH, Grok or paid calls occurred. No code became
unreachable through this change.

  Same-owner and visible-agent AI/file/browser/terminal/group/pane changes latch
  stale selection, including away-and-back. Agent, Group Chat and modal transitions,
  caller invalidation and unmount reject late true results.
- Assertions preserve the full current session/tab/log snapshot and history
  selector, including a newer tab with a new message created while loading.
  Stale completions cannot activate the owner, close Director Notes or clear Group
  Chat. Candidate-specific caller rejection receives the exact proposed object.
- Exact admitted existing and tiled focus objects succeed for Grok and Claude;
  ordinary metadata changes remain valid. A distinct object committing the same
  focus is refused. Newer requests supersede earlier callbacks; settling an older
  request does not dispose the newer request's subscriptions. Success, refusal,
  rejection, replacement and unmount dispose the three synchronous subscriptions,
  with replacement/unmount checked before late completion.

Independent static review found no blocking issue. The newer-tab sentinel was
added following review. Actual canonical read/commit/history-selector guards
remain **GT1-04-07CT**; **GT1-04-13** executes these suites. No tests were run or
collected, as this TEST task explicitly requires. No real account reads, SSH,
provider calls or paid turns occurred.

## Discovered repair and execution handoff

Independent static review identified an earlier race in `recall()`: it awaits
`getRecent(chatId)` without a request/current-chat generation check before
`endChat()` and adoption. A stale saved-record read can therefore overwrite a new
chat or newer completed recall before native reopen guards apply. Two ordinary,
unskipped regression cases assert the intended behavior and are **expected to
fail the current source**; this is a static finding, not an executed test result.
Added **GT1-04-07CQT1A** immediately next in the canonical playbook to repair this
bounded engine issue before GT1-04-13 runs both suites. No implementation was
folded into this TEST packet. The six affected parity rows retain their acceptance
statuses/applicability and now name the repair blocker.

# GT1-04-07D desktop launch ownership

Source: `cd8b4181542553f91fa7d0195c24be91d7516249` before this task's commit.
Execution root/branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`; HEAD is **230 ahead,
10 behind** `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`, whose merge base remains
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`. No branch/base edits.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[01]], [[02]], [[03]]. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and boundaries

Three existing production files, **466 changed lines** after formatting:

- `handle-spawn.ts` captures the request and exact retained owner/tab before
  discovery awaits, reusing `findOwningSessionRecord` and the existing tab-ID
  resolver. It resolves account ownership from the actual launch environment
  through canonical environment/account helpers and directory observations.
  Local relative GROK_HOME resolves against the child cwd; an inherited empty
  value selects the native default. Whitespace ambiguity, nonabsolute native
  cwd, missing account roots and ambiguous home defaults remain unknown.
- Remote capture uses the actual planned/wrapped remote, literal remote/global/
  provider/session environment precedence, `observePathRemote`, and the qualified
  endpoint installation identity. It never substitutes an SSH alias, local home,
  or process epoch. Endpoint qualification surrounds path observation; final
  qualification/path receipt checks repeat after admission, followed by a settings
  and tab ownership check. Managed endpoint disagreement refuses the launch.
- `types.ts` exposes `NativeSessionLaunchScope` without inventing a native ID,
  and `NativeSessionLaunch` with immutable scope, process generation, owning tab
  and requested native ID. `grokLaunchArgsProblem` validates the actual native
  resume selector and cwd before SSH wrapping and at the local sink. Conflicting,
  repeated, implicit, injected-ID, fork and worktree selectors are refused.
- `ProcessManager.ts` compares the retained binding with the observed scope and
  requested ID before spawning. Its host-owned `beforeSpawn` fence runs only
  after standalone/admitted outcomes; cancellation, refusal and unexpected-check
  exceptions release any unused lease. Local paths and inherited account env
  are checked synchronously at this boundary. Settings are not consulted when
  output arrives. Existing child/exit generation fences remain unchanged.
- The manager copies mutable Grok input before admission and freezes the
  registered launch scope beside `spawnGeneration`. A separate small registry
  survives `kill()` removing the live map entry, until the real exit follows
  final output drainage. `nativeSessionLaunchOf(sessionId)` exposes the captured
  generation to the session-ID listener, including an unterminated final line.
  No account content, credentials or output reparsing supplies this identity.

Independent static review identified and the packet repaired selector bypass,
managed endpoint disagreement, admission-time path changes, remote cwd ambiguity,
endpoint changes during the final reads and unnecessary guards after denial.
Final review found no blocking issue. Static reasoning does not qualify an actual
Grok binary or prove remote filesystem atomicity between independent SSH requests.

## Bounded continuation and test handoff

The three-file boundary leaves remote inherited/default/tilde account observation
for **GT1-04-07D1**, followed by **07D1T**, inserted before 07E. Current remote
binding requires an explicit absolute account root, absolute native cwd and a
qualified endpoint; unavailable standalone endpoint identity stays unknown.
Unknown required ownership refuses resume without starting a replacement session.
Missing account directories are never created merely to obtain provenance.

**GT1-04-07E** must synchronously read `nativeSessionLaunchOf` when handling the
existing session-ID event, validate the observed ID against its captured request,
then deliver/persist binding and generation to the owning tab. The registry is
cleared on final exit; asynchronous listeners must retain the returned immutable
value before yielding. 07E must not infer ownership from current settings or
assume the standalone ACP port is already desktop routing. **07F** retains runtime
and History ownership. No event/renderer payload is changed by this packet.

**GT1-04-08** owns general process test authoring in the existing handler,
ProcessManager and managed-admission suites: two colliding account/tab IDs,
unknown/malformed/wrong-provider retained bindings, actual selector mismatches
including wrapped SSH, selected account absence, local blank/default/relative
roots, symlink retargeting during admission, endpoint/config changes, store/tab
replacement during final remote reads, refused/throwing guards and lease release,
cancellation, no checks after denial, immutable generation capture, final line
with/without newline after kill, and superseded completion after successor exit.
Use inert helpers/processes and isolated paths; preserve legacy non-Grok behavior.
The inserted 07D1T owns remote environment-observer edge tests.
