# GT1-04-07D1 remote launch account observation

Source: `d75a4f5506d33365a0fae0b7d9069851fc759c76` before this task's commit.
Execution root and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`. HEAD is
**231 ahead, 10 behind** `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`; merge base remains
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`. No branch/base changes.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[01]], [[02]], [[03]]. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and pinned native semantics

Two existing production files, **179 changed lines** after formatting:

- `remote-fs.ts` extends the existing bounded `observePathRemote` and its Python
  directory-identity observer. Its optional Grok launch mode models only HOME and
  GROK_HOME, with absent values inherited from the remote login environment and
  configured values retained literally. It uses the existing admission, SSH
  concurrency/retry limits, command timeout, Python 25-second alarm, shell/path
  escaping, metadata validation and 100,000-character receipt bound. Legacy
  path observation and descriptor reads keep their existing contracts.
- The shell cd uses the remote login HOME before selected remote/global/provider/
  session exports. The native `--cwd` is then applied literally, and the headless
  session cwd is separately canonicalized. A relative `--cwd r` can therefore
  select process cwd `login/r/r` and session cwd `login/r/r/r`; account roots are
  interpreted under the process cwd. Literal `~` in CLI/env values is not expanded.
- Empty/unset GROK_HOME selects the default; whitespace is not empty. Empty/unset
  HOME uses the remote OS user home, matching pinned Rust behavior. The default
  home is canonicalized before `.grok` is appended. The observer never creates
  a missing selected root and never falls through to another Grok account.
- `handle-spawn.ts` passes the validated final native cwd selector and the actual
  merged remote launch environment to observation. It keeps the full private
  receipt for its existing admission recheck: raw inherited/effective HOME and
  GROK_HOME plus shell/process/session/account path, device and inode identities.
  Qualified endpoint checks surround both samples; existing settings/tab fences
  run again after asynchronous checks. Unknown ownership still refuses resume.
- The account reference still uses the canonical resolver. Canonical paths with
  trailing whitespace or literal backslashes remain unknown because the shared
  account/binding wire would normalize them into a different spelling. A literal
  value is never rewritten merely to obtain a bound receipt.

Independent static review found a default HOME alias race; the final observer
retains and rechecks that original HOME path as well as the native process-cwd
path. No credential file, conversation content or provider process is consulted.
These are finite observations, not atomic guarantees across separate SSH sessions.
Missing Python, selected directories or qualified endpoint identity stays unavailable.

Pinned Grok source is `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8`:

- [CLI apply_cwd](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/app/cli.rs#L838-L855), SHA-256 `bd614658c9a20922497951d98e2e49f2a4c94bc247389ee79b39b0d00728022e`.
- [Startup ordering and unchanged headless cwd argument](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager-bin/src/main.rs#L2067-L2088), SHA-256 `4886fcb1e5f18690cac13e237f0f0ebf19101c629d625d829d22831f0dee9b66`.
- [Headless cwd canonicalization](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/headless.rs#L803-L814), SHA-256 `4ee686a891b2f650a4db511125694c711861e0195eb680dd3131553b76a005c3`.
- [Native account path rules](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-dirs/src/lib.rs#L41-L70), SHA-256 `b047017fd856744eeee09fecb6abc38399982c82ecac0c42572e372ee53df665`.
- [Pinned Rust 1.94.0 Unix home fallback](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/blob/1.94.0/library/std/src/sys/pal/unix/os.rs#L480-L484), SHA-256 `df9d59ba3042ed5dc3aa62c1bebefd1000cc511f40bc9a2642fb26e29ffab008`.

## Authored coverage

Extended only `src/__tests__/main/utils/remote-fs.test.ts`: **277 changed lines**
after formatting, **28 new parameter-expanded test cases**. Eight receipt-field
cases each check both the exact limit and overflow. Reused the existing inert
SSH dependencies, repository-owned fixture tree, emitted Python and syscall
audit instead of adding another harness or invoking an installed provider.

- Fourteen emitted-program cases cover absent/empty HOME with stubbed passwd
  homes (valid, empty, missing entry and nonexistent directory), empty passwd
  fallback from the applied native process cwd, explicit selected accounts with
  unavailable home, and HOME/native-cwd symlinks retargeted after initial samples.
  Retargets require `identity-mismatch`, one injected mutation and no receipt.
- The passwd lookup never calls the real user's lookup. Child environments and
  metadata paths are confined to fixtures. Python `open` audit events catch
  built-in and descriptor file opens; every audited launch asserts zero content
  opens, reads, bytes, descriptors and writes. Synthetic credential/transcript
  sentinels replace real account contents. Deliberate alias faults are tracked
  separately from observer writes.
- Launch receipt controls accept exactly 100,000 ASCII bytes and reject 100,001;
  all four canonical paths and four environment fields check 16,384/16,385 limits.
  Four fake-timer cases cover recovery, retry exhaustion and ETIMEDOUT, asserting
  500/1,000/2,000 ms backoff, at most four attempts, identical commands, discarded
  failed receipts and no leftover timers or real SSH transport.

The audited launch cases extract the actual emitted Python from the Bash wrapper
only for an absolute fixture cwd. They do not emulate shell path semantics. The
existing real Bash shell-cd versus literal-export controls are retained.
Independent static review found no blocking issue; its suggested exhausted-retry
error assertion was added. ProcessManager generation/resume coverage remains

## Implementation and bounded handoff

Three existing production files, **45 changed lines** after formatting:

- `src/main/process-listeners/session-id-listener.ts` reads the existing
  `nativeSessionLaunchOf` synchronously before group-chat delegation or renderer
  delivery. It forwards the captured receipt with the observed native ID, retaining
  requested ID, account/host/cwd, owning tab and process generation separately.
- `src/main/preload/process/core.ts` carries the optional third argument unchanged;
  `src/renderer/global.d.ts` declares the same canonical `NativeSessionLaunch`
  through a type-only import. No new helper, identity type or history store.
- Missing receipts preserve the exact two-argument event/callback shape. Unknown
  scope stays unknown. Neither bridge consults current settings, invents IDs nor
  replaces a mismatched observed ID with the requested one. The consumer needs
  both IDs to retain the original history and surface its existing refusal.

Independent review found no blocking issue within this bridge packet. Completing
all four identified files would exceed CONTRACT's three-file maximum, so the
immediately following **GT1-04-07E1** owns durable renderer persistence and current
turn checks. The existing consumer currently ignores the new third argument.
There is no renderer current-generation baseline yet: comparing only previously
received ID generations cannot reject every old IPC event queued before a new
spawn. The continuation must establish that fence through the existing lifecycle,
reject changed tab ownership, and atomically persist the observed binding with
`updateProviderSlot`. A real ACP allocation and native headless end must obey the
same eventual contract, but the standalone ACP port still has no desktop routing.

`src/main/process-listeners/__tests__/session-id-listener.test.ts` and

`nativeSessionLaunchOf` to the existing listener mock; without it the old
forwarding cases will fail at runtime. No production fallback is added merely to
accommodate an incomplete test double. **GT1-04-07E1T** owns the existing renderer
listener suite and affected lifecycle/persistence coverage. **GT1-04-13** executes
all of them, including the colocated path outside `src/__tests__`. General process
generation tests remain GT1-04-08; runtime and History propagation remain 07F.

# GT1-04-07E1 native launch request correlation

Source: `dc81dc4b9a55ee14439c18c3a604f24f08f83181` before this task's commit.
Execution root and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`. HEAD is
**235 ahead, 10 behind** `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`; merge base is
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`. No branch/base changes.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[01]], [[02]], [[03]]. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implemented prerequisite and bounded continuation

Three existing production files, **25 added lines** after formatting:

- `process-manager/types.ts` and `process/spawn-types.ts` declare optional
  `nativeSessionRequestId` on the launch request and immutable native receipt.
  It is a caller correlation token, never native identity or managed authority.
- `ProcessManager.ts` captures the scalar before admission/spawner callbacks,
  validates 1-128 ASCII letters/digits/underscore/hyphen without trimming or
  coercion, and echoes it only on a successful Grok launch receipt. Missing or
  invalid tokens omit the property; no token is invented. A trailing newline is
  invalid. Non-Grok receipts and legacy callers retain their existing behavior.
- Existing `handle-spawn.ts` structured cloning before discovery and
  `spawnAdmitted()` copying before admission already retain the token through
  waits. The existing third-argument bridge forwards the captured receipt.
  Neither argv/env nor account scope, native IDs or admission snapshots derive
  from this field. Kill/drain receipt retention is unchanged.

Independent static investigation and diff review found no blocking issue in this
prerequisite. It also found that `codifyTurnSettings` runs during render and
settings projection, so it cannot mint a turn baseline. No usable renderer
current-generation baseline currently exists. A token must be minted at the
preload spawn boundary and announced synchronously before invoking IPC.

The necessary schema, host capture, preload lifecycle, declaration and renderer
consumer span more than three production files. Under CONTRACT, **07E1A** is now
inserted immediately before dependent tests with the three remaining concrete
files: preload `process/core.ts`, `global.d.ts`, and
`useAgentSessionIdListener.ts`. It must establish exact tab/attempt ownership,
validate requested/retained IDs and captured scope, and call `updateProviderSlot`
atomically. Reuse `matchesNativeSessionBinding` for bound-scope validation.
Cancellation alone must not invalidate a confirmed native allocation; a successor
attempt must invalidate the previous baseline even before its first ID arrives.
Removed/replaced tabs, non-Grok successors, failed/colliding attempts, cleanup,
reload and missing/unknown lifecycle receipts need explicit handling.

**No renderer fencing or durable binding write is claimed in this packet.**
The consumer still ignores the receipt. Standalone ACP is not desktop routing.
**07E1PT** owns the two existing host regression suites; **07ET/07E1T** own the
preload/consumer coverage; **07F** retains runtime/History propagation; **GT1-04-13**
executes the phase suites. No tests were run or collected.

# GT1-04-07E1A desktop native attempt fencing and persistence

Source: `66f4e3f0a665331584fd435618a560ec51416daa` before this task's commit.
Execution root/branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`. HEAD is **236 ahead,
10 behind** `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`, with merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`. No branch/base changes.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[01]], [[02]], [[03]]. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and review

Three existing production files, **296 changed lines** after formatting:

- Preload `process/core.ts` mints a canonical UUID plus a monotonic suffix for
  each nonterminal attempt. It copies config before callbacks and announces a
  frozen scalar snapshot before IPC. A synchronous FIFO preserves notification
  and invocation order under reentrant spawns without waiting for IPC results.
  Unsubscribed callbacks are skipped; a throwing callback does not prevent other
  listeners fencing the attempt. Clone/callback failures refuse IPC. Failed or
  colliding attempts never restore a predecessor; terminal behavior is unchanged.
- `global.d.ts` declares optional `onSpawnAttempt` with the preload type. No
  replay occurs on subscription or reload. Older/web bridges cannot establish a
  Grok baseline. This callback is local lifecycle plumbing, not a new IPC channel.
- `useAgentSessionIdListener.ts` captures the exact agent/tab lifetime, original
  provider slot and requested native ID. Enumerable symbol markers survive normal
  immutable spreads and parking but disappear through JSON/structured cloning;
  synchronous store subscription also invalidates removed/replaced ownership.
  Successor AI attempts invalidate the tab even before their first ID arrives,
  including non-Grok and forced-parallel attempts. Separate synopsis/batch jobs
  do not invalidate other tabs. Kill, interrupt and exit are not supersession.
- Grok receipt consumption requires the current token/tab, a positive safe process
  generation, matching captured request and valid bound scope. Binding fields come
  only from captured host provenance plus the observed native ID; canonical
  `matchesNativeSessionBinding` checks scope and retained ownership. ID-only or
  unknown retained slots cannot be promoted. Mismatched requested/retained IDs
  retain history and use the existing error path without corrupting the incoming
  provider. `updateProviderSlot` writes ID and binding atomically to live/parked
  state, with the expected slot advanced before the synchronous store notification.

Independent review found and the packet repaired utility-spawn over-invalidation,
reentrant callback reversal and callback-failure notification gaps. No further
supported-path blocker was found. These are static findings, not executed proof.
Legacy non-Grok event handling remains unchanged; an old bridge without either
lifecycle or receipt provenance cannot retrospectively attribute indistinguishable
cross-provider events. No Grok binding is reconstructed from current settings.

then cover synchronous notification, UUID collisions, config mutation, reentrant
order, callback failure/unsubscription, clone/invoke failure and terminal controls.
**GT1-04-07E1T** owns existing consumer tests, including ordinary raw ai-tab process
IDs without config.tabId, same-ID replacement, remove/readd, two tabs/accounts,
parking, utility spawns, unknown receipts, malformed scope/generation, request/slot
mismatch, changed settings, failed successors, cancellation drainage and cleanup.
**GT1-04-13** executes these suites. Runtime/History propagation stays with 07F;
standalone ACP is still not desktop routing or real native qualification.

are reused. The small listener announcement fixture reads no current settings.

- `useAgentSessionIdListener.test.tsx` covers exact account/endpoint/cwd binding,
  native end-only identity, ACP allocation, confirmed cancellation-time allocation,
  absent/invalid IDs, repeat identity and raw ai-tab callers without config.tabId.
  Two colliding IDs remain isolated across accounts/tabs after settings and provider
  changes. Synchronous store observations require atomic ID/binding writes into
  parked Grok slots while preserving the incoming provider and original history.
- Missing, unknown and malformed receipt scope/token/tab/generation, mismatched
  requested/retained IDs, replaced/removed agent or tab ownership, changed creation
  identity/turn provider/native slot, reconstructed bindings and remove/readd all
  preserve retained data. Requested and retained ID failures are independently
  exercised in both live and parked slots. Unknown legacy/unobserved ownership
  cannot become bound through matching IDs or selected settings.
- Queued predecessor events are rejected before a successor emits any ID,
  including non-Grok, forced-parallel, invalid-tab, legacy and bare attempts. A
  valid successor can then establish its own generation; utility jobs do not
  invalidate another tab. Repeated receipts pin the accepted generation. Unmount
  removes both bridge subscriptions, and reload/older bridges cannot rebuild a
  baseline from an arriving ID.
- `useDebouncedPersistence.test.ts` applies late bindings via `updateProviderSlot`
  to two parked Grok sessions with colliding native IDs. Real debounced persistence
  flush/merge, JSON reload and provider restoration preserve exact account scopes,
  incoming Codex state, old snapshots and original history. Existing persistence
  variants already cover absent/unknown bindings and snoozed slots.

Independent static review found no remaining behavioral issue and confirmed that
normal immutable spreads preserve incarnation markers while reconstruction does
not. Compiler checks found and corrected parameter-table tuple inference. These
are authored assertions and static findings, not executed runtime proof.
**GT1-04-13** owns execution, **GT1-04-08** broader host generations, and

qualification remain **not-run**. Only GT1-04-07E1T was processed.

# GT1-04-07F runtime native recording prerequisites

Source HEAD before this task: `78f9fd66bd759fc4d53a32d08526daa51ace9544`.
Checkout and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and run-state. Base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` remains
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, ahead/behind **240/10**;
the base is not an ancestor of HEAD (exit 1). No ancestry changes were made.
Read the phase 01 pinned schema evidence, phase 02 account/environment rules,
phase 03 observed native identity rules and existing phase 04 handoffs. Images
analyzed: **0**. No native account contents, credentials, Grok process or SSH
connection was accessed; live authorization remains disabled with zero budget.

## Implemented boundary

Only three production files changed, **172 changed lines** after formatting:

- `agents/rules.ts` extends existing `TurnBegin`/`TabSessionUpdate` and
  `recordTabSession`. A runtime attempt marker is separate from native identity;
  optional expected turn/ID/binding checks execute inside the repository mutation
  queue, including check-only requests and parked provider slots. Binding and ID
  travel atomically through existing `updateProviderSlot`, with detached binding
  objects. Existing callers without expectations retain their behavior.
- `runtime/turns.ts` snapshots original project cwd, remote and worktree
  attribution before launch. It validates raw Grok ID events, including the
  same-ID resume announcement suppressed by the mapper. Invalid or different
  IDs become failed outcomes before journal/History success and cannot replace
  the retained ID or fold usage into it. A genuinely observed early allocation
  survives cancellation and a missing terminal ID; a requested resume ID alone
  is never fabricated as a terminal observation. ID events follow the queued
  persistence attempt, so a rejected write does not announce success.
- `turns/record-turn.ts` detaches supplied attribution before awaiting writers,
  uses original scope for History/stats and accepts observed bindings for future
  admitted receipts. A mismatched supplied bound identity cannot make successful
  History. Grok History without a receipt carries explicit unknown provenance;
  current settings never become a native binding.

This packet deliberately records new runtime IDs as **unobserved**, not bound.
Original project attribution is not proof of canonical native cwd/account/host.
Existing recorded bindings are not sufficient to validate a new launch by ID.
No dead production path was made unreachable and no new storage backend, matcher,
account resolver, SSH command builder or process runner was introduced.

## Bounded remaining ownership

The initial three starting files cannot implement the complete invariant:
repository rules dropped bindings, the canonical observer/selector validator and
matcher reside in desktop modules, and `AgentTurnRun` has no admitted receipt.
CONTRACT's packet boundary therefore splits the remaining concrete work into
[[GROK-TIER1-04]] tasks **07F1/07F2/07F3** (reuse/extract existing shared
primitives), **07F4** (production host observation, immutable admitted receipt and
pre-resume validation), and **07F5** (receipt consumer plus full lifetime fence).

The current final ID comparison precedes asynchronous transcript/History writes;
it does not yet fence replacement during those writes or replacement before
`beginTurn`. **07F5** must check at the actual mutations. Unknown launch scope,
changed endpoints and retained binding validation remain **07F4**, not claimed
complete by this packet. Native ACP runtime routing remains unqualified.

**07FT** owns regression authoring in the existing runtime turns, recorder and
agent rules suites. **07FPT**, **07F4T** and **07F5T** own each continuation's
bounded tests. **GT1-04-13** executes all of them. No tests were authored, run or
collected here, as this IMPLEMENT task explicitly prohibits test execution and
separates authoring. Deterministic and live qualification remain **not-run**.
Independent static review found no blocking issue in the limited prerequisite
scope and confirmed the remaining ownership above.

Only the three permitted production files changed (425 changed lines after
formatting). Existing scope observer/matcher, provider-slot CAS, repository
mutation queue, History writer and stats recorder are reused:

- `runtime/turns.ts` consumes the immutable admitted launch receipt. Genuine early
  allocations and end-only IDs are persisted with their observed account/host/cwd
  binding in one `recordTabSession` update. Same-ID resumes still validate the
  complete observed binding. Unknown/no receipt never becomes observed provenance;
  a requested ID alone never becomes an observed terminal ID. Cancellation retains
  an actual allocation. History/stats use the original canonical receipt scope.
- `agents/repository.ts` captures an in-memory tab incarnation independent of native
  IDs and settings. Ordinary edits/parking retain it; committed remove/recreate and
  reload invalidate it even when IDs collide. Optional guards execute after queue
  waits and before mutation. `withTabWriteGuard` reserves the existing queue across
  the external writers' internal queues and filesystem awaits; it cannot nest
  queued repository methods. Reload now uses that same queue. This prevents a
  successor mutation from interleaving with a record already being committed.
- `turns/record-turn.ts` passes the original guard to transcript append and reserves
  ownership for History/stats. Removed/replaced/superseded ownership cannot write
  successful replacement history or usage. Lifecycle failures and final runtime
  outcomes reflect refused recording; managed terminal journaling follows records.
  Non-Grok/legacy callers keep optional guards absent and retain existing behavior.

Independent static review found and corrected the missing-tab lifecycle projection
(which could otherwise claim completion after removal), then found no remaining
blocking defect. Original scope/ownership is never reconstructed from current
settings or native ID equality. The queue protects repository-owned mutations;
callers must use repository operations rather than mutate returned records in place.

parking, changed settings, removal/recreation before begin, successors during
queued transcript/History writes, failed resume, cancellation and missing/end-only
ID regressions. It must replace the old unknown-binding expectations in 07FT/07F4T
and the obsolete `rejected same-ID check` spy with actual queued-mutation assertions.

# GT1-04-08A native fork and rename boundaries

Pre-task HEAD: `293e5598d9903ddb69d4635c51bdcb728be38f10`. Checkout and branch
match [[CONTRACT]] and run-state. Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` resolves to
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; ahead/behind is **251/10**, merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, and base ancestry check exits **1**.
No ancestry changes. Related: [[GROK-TIER1-04]], [[GROK-TIER1-05]], [[DESIGN]].
Images analyzed: **0**.

## Reference behavior and native evidence

The shared Claude/Codex fork hook builds a new prompt from displayed user/assistant/
tool text and allocates a fresh tab. It is a context fork: it does not duplicate
native history, reasoning, attachment structure or tool ancestry. Grok's displayed
log index is not a verified native `targetPromptIndex`. The generic rename IPC
writes only Maestro origins metadata, keyed by provider/project/native ID; it does
not change the provider title and cannot distinguish colliding accounts or hosts.

Pinned public source `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` includes native
`x.ai/session/fork` and `x.ai/session/rename` extension dispatch. Existing local
receipts were re-read at `tmp/grok-tier1-protocol/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/agent/mvp_agent/acp_agent.rs:2035`
and `tmp/grok-tier1-protocol/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/headless.rs:581`.
The headless fork consumes the returned new native ID before opening its child.
Title validation has manual/reset modes and provider limits in the existing
`tmp/grok-tier1-04-05-schema/xai-grok-shell/src/session/persistence.rs:52` receipt.
Independent audit also inspected the pinned public
[session administration source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/extensions/session_admin.rs)
and [fork source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/session/fork.rs):
native title changes coordinate title locks/generation, resident state and search;
native fork coordinates parent identity, branch position and copied native data.
These are source observations, not candidate 1.0.70 binary qualification.

Maestro's `src/shared/maestro-lib/grok/acp-session.ts` admits only qualified
prompt-ID/model-list/session-usage extensions. No verified mutation route exists.
Direct JSONL/summary writes would bypass native consistency, so the storage adapter
remains read-only for these operations. No unused mutation API or new backend was
introduced. Native fork and native title change are **unsupported through Maestro**,
while native/live qualification remains **not-run**; this is not a claim that Grok
itself lacks these operations.

## Implemented boundary and remaining consumer work

- The fork hook refuses source or target Grok before creating a tab, preparing a
  prompt or spawning. It reuses `resolveTurnProvider` so retained Grok ownership
  survives provider changes, and reports busy/allocation-pending state separately.
  The notice describes native CLI fork and distinguishes prompt context copying.
  Original logs, selected branch point, account/cwd/host, ancestry and native ID
  stay untouched; no replacement or guessed child ID is created.
- Grok `setSessionName` rejects before store access, including missing-store and
  null/reset requests. Its error explains that local tab labels and native titles
  are different, and offers the native CLI/refresh path. Other providers retain
  their existing origins behavior.
- Grok origins reads suppress only ambiguous session names without mutating saved
  metadata. Named-session origins, default-storage History attribution and mirror
  fallbacks exclude Grok; bound Grok History cannot be assigned to another storage.
  Unscoped star/origin metadata and legacy unbound History attribution remain
  outside this label-only claim. Existing stored data is not removed.

Independent review found the tab rename paths in `useSessionLifecycle.ts` and
`useRemoteIntegration.ts` still issue global `history.updateSessionName(nativeId)`
regardless of origins success; this can rename histories from colliding accounts.
The History browser also logs rename rejection without user-visible feedback.
These three consumers exceed this task's named-file boundary. Added bounded
**GT1-04-08A1** and **08A1T** immediately before **08B**, with concrete files and
invariants. They remain unchecked. Do not claim end-to-end rename isolation until
that continuation and its tests pass. Other legacy label callers can receive the
explicit IPC refusal; no native title success is returned.

## Native contract and implemented refusal

Pinned official source at `2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` exposes
whole-session delete and prompt-index rewind. Native deletion tears down resident
writers/children, performs authoritative remote deletion before local deletion,
and evicts the search index. Rewind removes the selected prompt and later prompts,
requires a resident session and defaults to rolling back files too. Neither is
an arbitrary message-pair edit. Sources: [session administration](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/extensions/session_admin.rs)
and [rewind](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell/src/extensions/rewind.rs).
The local `session/acp_types.rs` receipt has SHA-256
`191c16295b4669b2aa5f20dc5681208c06179c4fd3c7ea76670300b8f3407eb7`;
`agent/mvp_agent/acp_agent.rs` has
`b1bc946841aef05059062de760fbaa0f46edf68f40304905b621874e0d4a56e5`.
Maestro's `src/shared/maestro-lib/grok/acp-session.ts` admits only qualified
prompt-ID, model-list and session-usage extensions. No verified native mutation
transaction can be invoked through this consumer API.

Three production files changed, **38 insertions and 1 deletion**:

- `useScrollLogHandlers` recognizes current Grok, retained Grok turn ownership
  through `resolveTurnProvider`, and parked Grok session state. It gives an
  actionable unsupported toast and returns `null` synchronously, before Claude
  IPC, log removal, command-history changes or a scroll target. The same refusal
  applies without a native ID, while busy, and with missing/changed account or
  remote configuration, without reading any provider history. Local shell-command
  cards and terminal history keep their existing local deletion path.
- Independent review found that the latest turn owner alone missed old Grok logs
  after a new Claude turn. The parked-slot guard closes that gap. Mixed-provider
  tabs conservatively refuse even newer non-Grok rows while retaining Grok state,
  because rendered logs lack per-row native ownership. No guessed native IDs,
  account defaults or fallback text are sent to a mutation API.
- Generic `agentSessions:deleteMessagePair` returns `success: false` for Grok
  before storage lookup. Its old request has no selected owner/account/host or
  native generation receipt. The adapter remains an inert refusal with a clear
  transaction/index explanation. No JSONL, summary, events, usage or FTS write
  is introduced, and no native mutation success is reported.

Consumer inspection found no separate native message-edit or whole-session-delete
path in the named surface. The Markdown display toggle only changes presentation;
queue editing affects unsent messages. Removing a Maestro agent is not a native
history mutation. These distinctions do not qualify native deletion/editing.
P14 and REG-SESSION-MUTATION remain required/pending with an explicit
`grok-native-delete-edit-transport-unqualified` blocker and a receipt linking
this refusal. Other statuses, blockers and evidence applicability are preserved.

## Implementation and limits

Changed only `src/main/ipc/handlers/context.ts` and
`src/renderer/utils/contextExtractor.ts` (**194 changed lines**). Reused
`SessionMessagesResult`, `SessionReadOptions`, `matchesNativeSessionBinding`,
`canonicalJson`, `redactSecrets`, and the existing account/host-aware
`agentSessions.read` IPC/storage path. No second native parser or store was added.

- `extractStoredSessionContext` requires an owning Maestro agent for Grok and
  requests `includeNativeRecords` through the existing observed history boundary.
  That boundary resolves/rechecks the chosen account, cwd and host, forwards the
  storage binding, and rejects stale/mismatched ownership. The extractor also
  compares an optional retained binding and refuses content without observed
  ownership. Legacy unscoped `context:getStoredSession` refuses Grok before
  storage access; other providers retain their old retrieval path.
- A single bounded read returns at most the latest **1,000 normalized records**
  in source order. There is no stitching of pages across native generations.
  `hasMore`/total visibly disclose earlier omitted records; absent/partial source
  provenance produces a missing-content notice even when no messages are returned.
  Only verified ownership plus available source can assert an empty transcript.
- Logs preserve role/type, stable source-line identity, tool-call/result linkage,
  source issues and image references as redacted inert text. No historical tools
  run and no referenced image paths/URLs open. Missing timestamps remain unknown
  (`NaN` in the existing in-memory LogEntry shape plus explicit text), without
  clock invention or sorting by absent dates.
- Visible normalized reasoning is opt-in. Raw native payloads and opaque
  reasoning are always omitted because assistant/unknown rows can embed legacy
  reasoning under other fields. Unknown/backend rows whose detailed evidence only
  exists in raw retain provenance and an explicit omission notice; this projection
  is not a native archive. Image bytes/references are retained as text without
  interpretation, not exposed as renderer image-load requests.
- A module-private provenance-log ID identifies these context projections for the
  existing formatters; it grants no ownership, resume or migration authority.
  Grooming preserves historical tool/file contents instead of replacing them with
  a claim that the file is currently available on disk. Clipboard retains source,
  tool and omission notices and independently hides thinking unless opted in.
  Final formatted text is redacted again through the canonical helper.

Independent static review found and resolved the unverified-empty wording and
historical-file stripping issues, then passed the final production diff. The
source/data format baseline remains pinned Grok public source
`2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8` and candidate package 1.0.70;
this is not exact-binary or remote-host certification.

Neither stored-context retrieval entry has a current production UI caller.
**GT1-06-01** already owns UI/CLI export and merge consumers; this task implements
and guards the normalization boundary, not a new visible workflow. Clipboard's
ordinary dialogue mode remains unchanged when there is no context-provenance
marker. Capability flags, native translation directions and all acceptance statuses
remain unchanged. P14/P21/C-supportsContextExport retain explicit verification and
consumer-wiring blockers. Native resume/migration remain separate contracts.

## Changes and limits

- `src/main/stats/token-usage/token-usage-accessor.ts` retains discovered account
  roots, keys Grok observations by account/cwd/native ID and skips duplicate
  directory observations. Grok ignores the transcript-size/summary-time cache
  because usage-only changes, corruption or deletion need not change that
  fingerprint. The ordinary 30-second collection memo remains. Partial coverage
  now survives aggregation; unknown dates do not become today's timeline entry.
  Ambiguous native-ID-only per-tab series omit conflicting owners while aggregate
  account/project totals remain. No throughput value is invented.
- `src/main/cue/stats/cue-token-accessor.ts` now returns and caches by the
  Maestro/provider ID pair through `sessionTokenLookupKey`, reusing canonicalJson.
  Lifecycle is resolved before cache reuse, and cache keys include recorded
  provider/project/remote metadata. Grok returns partial/null-cost results without
  cache or native reads: Cue rows contain no observed account/host receipt, and
  current settings cannot recover one. The static Grok full-support declaration
  remains unchanged; it does not override observation-level missingness.
- The third existing storage consumer is `src/main/cue/stats/cue-stats-query.ts`.
  All three consumers (totals, chains, timeline) use pair keys and event-stream
  usage for identified Grok runs, never native lifetime totals. Unknown/partial
  structured cost and legacy Grok or unidentified numeric projections stay null;
  exact ticks use usageCostUsd. Mixed known/unknown contributions stay unknown.
  Original accounting/model detail remains attached; invalid token counts and
  native-session/child snapshots cannot become turn deltas. Missing native IDs
  still yield a visible partial-coverage warning for identified Grok agents.

This is **not** complete usage qualification. The numeric-only dashboard contract
still loses raw model/unknown-cost detail; Cue's existing converter drops accounting;
creation-time lifecycle provider labels cannot identify turns after provider swaps;
account enumeration misses global-shell/tilde precedence. Under CONTRACT's packet
rule, the canonical document now owns these concrete continuations before tests:
**11A** shared/dashboard representation, **11B** CLI/export consumers, **11C** Cue
accounting and observed provider receipts (including no-usage/no-ID runs), and
**11D** canonical account discovery. Native Cue history fallback remains unavailable
without an observed account/host receipt; no native scope is inferred from settings.
Existing phase 08/10/11 work must reuse these changes, not duplicate parsers/stores.

GT1-04-12 owns accessor regressions, including pair-key migration and usage-only
cache invalidation. Added 12A owns the changed query mock/helper and producer
regressions; 12B owns UI/account-discovery cases; 12C owns CLI/export cases.
GT1-04-13 executes all suites. P11/P16/P40/REG-USAGE/C-supportsUsageStats/
C-supportsCostTracking/S26 retain their original acceptance statuses and applicability,
with explicit pending continuation owners and blockers. No quota collector, pricing
implementation, database, account read, provider execution or live claim was added.

# GT1-04-11A Dashboard accounting

Implemented on 2026-10-31 from `4c96e1a9a30141087ae4a11e7f3682d02f48da1f`.
The execution root and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and run-state. Base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`,
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **260/10**.
No ancestry changes. Unrelated `.sloptimize/` is preserved.

Read [[01]] pinned schema receipts, [[02]] account-layer rules, [[03]] native
identity/accounting evidence, [[GROK-TIER1-04]], and the provider, platform,
shared-utility, storage, dashboard and development guides. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation

- The existing shared token-usage shapes carry full `UsageAccounting` on sessions,
  `UsageBreakdown` plus raw identity on model rows, and canonical cost/completeness
  on aggregate groups and timeline buckets. Aggregate metadata does not claim one
  native session owns a total spanning multiple sessions. Numeric fields remain
  compatible projections, and pure legacy groups retain their prior presentation.
- Native lifetime totals are added once to grand, agent, project, account and
  timeline totals. `accounting.models` supplies detail even when storage withholds
  legacy `byModel`; partial model splits and completed-child detail never replace
  or add to the lifetime total. Raw model keys include the provider and bypass
  pricing normalization. Model group session counts count each session once.
- Reused `usageCostUsd` and `mergeUsageCost`. Exact tick strings survive aggregation;
  unknown/partial sources cannot become exact zero. Per-contribution legacy cost
  evidence remains available when canonical data arrives later, including a single
  legacy session with both estimated and reported model rows. Reported decimals
  pass through the canonical tick merger before summation to avoid the floating
  `0.1 + 0.2` subtotal changing the answer when provider enumeration order changes.
  Mixed estimated/reported totals retain only the known reported subtotal as
  partial; they do not relabel an estimate as a provider-reported charge.
- Dashboard cost cards, model/account/group rows, bar magnitudes and timeline
  tooltips use canonical cost evidence. Unknown is labelled `Unknown`, partial
  amounts are marked `(partial)`, and an explicit exact zero stays `$0.00`.
  Numeric projections and sorting match the canonical displayed amount.
- Existing account/cwd/native-ID deduplication, usage-only cache bypass, monotonic
  coverage, unknown timestamps and ambiguous native-ID series protections remain.
  No speed samples are synthesized; the existing no-timing empty state remains.

mixed-model costs, decimal subtotals preceding ticks, unknown/partial/exact-zero

Implemented on 2026-10-31 from `f46eb544f294565cbd6ac796a1d11d07ade46397`.

Read the project/development/provider/platform, storage, shared-utility and stats
guides, [[01]]/phase04 schema receipts, [[02]] account rules, [[03]] native identity
and accounting, parity, and the preceding 04-11/11A handoff. Images analyzed: **0**.

## Implementation and compatibility

Production scope is exactly **2 files, 79 changed lines** after formatting.

- `src/cli/commands/stats.ts` composes existing `usageCostUsd` and `formatCost`
  directly in its summary loop. Totals, provider, raw-model and account rows use
  canonical evidence before compatibility numbers: unknown stays `Unknown`,
  partial amounts carry `(partial)`, explicit exact zero remains `$0.00`, and
  legacy estimates retain `~`. Stable keys accompany labels when needed to
  distinguish accounts with the same basename or provider/model identities.
  Both cache buckets remain visible. Zero timed calls show `Unknown (no timed calls)`.
- `src/main/stats/usage-export.ts` appends the canonical `accounting` column to
  all five token CSV dimensions (provider, model, project, account, timeline).
  The existing CSV renderer serializes exact tick strings and completeness as
  JSON cells. A small typed projection reuses `usageCostUsd` to leave unavailable
  numeric costs empty and retain measured zero/partial amounts; canonical status
  also controls `costEstimated`. Input rows and original accounting are not mutated.
  Cue CSV now retains `streamUsageJson`, preserving any recorded model/cache/cost
  evidence without introducing another parser or native lookup.
- CLI JSON, full JSON export and CSV's `export-info.json` already preserve the
  entire canonical accounting object. Their established numeric compatibility
  fields remain unchanged, including a zero projection when cost is unknown;
  consumers must read `accounting.cost` before interpreting that projection.
  No fields are removed or renamed, so export version 1 remains compatible.
  Model detail is not re-summed into lifetime totals. No calculator, database,
  provider account read, native execution or capability claim was added.

Independent static review found no blocking behavioral issue. Main type checking
initially rejected the CSV generic column inference (exit 2); the projected key
signature was corrected and the final full main check passes. No tests were
written, run or collected: **GT1-04-12C** owns CLI/export regression authoring and
**GT1-04-13** owns execution. Those regressions should include unknown and
partial-without-amount cells, exact tick zero/nonzero, conflicting compatibility
projections, mixed-provider totals, all five CSV dimensions, input immutability,
colliding account labels/raw model keys, Cue stream preservation and legacy data.

# GT1-04-11C Cue stream accounting and provider identity

Implemented on 2026-10-31 from `478e69ac3b38ea90d5bdda5a54c2d2d415ce0b7a` in the configured execution checkout and
branch. Base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`, merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **262/10**. Neither
branch tip is an ancestor of the other. No ancestry or primary-checkout changes;
unrelated `.sloptimize/` stays excluded. Read [[CONTRACT]], [[GROK-TIER1-04]],
[[01]] schema receipts, [[02]] account rules, [[03]] native identity/accounting,
run-state, parity and the repository/provider/platform/Cue/stats guides.

## Implementation

Production scope: **3 files, 210 changed lines**.

- `cue-process-lifecycle.ts` reuses `parsedUsageToStats` and the scoped
  `UsageAccumulator.accumulate` path already used by `TurnCapture`. Turn snapshots
  replace covered response usage; repeated terminal reports do not add the same
  spend again. Exact tick strings, partial/unknown cost, both cache buckets,
  reasoning and per-model detail survive the existing `streamUsageJson` writer.
  Native/child and unproved process-session totals never become event spend.
- `UsageAccounting.providerId` records the host-selected provider for that run.
  Missing usage/native IDs, prelaunch stop/refusal and spawn failure produce a
  receipt with `source: none`, unknown completeness/cost and compatibility zeros.
  Such receipts do not prove that a process started and are never seeded into
  the accumulator, so they cannot contaminate later exact observations.
- `cue-stats-query.ts` parses the existing stored JSON once per event and uses
  observed provider identity before creation-time lifecycle labels. Totals,
  agent groups, chain nodes, time buckets and coverage use the same event maps.
  Observed receipts are excluded before native lookup, even when malformed usage
  fails projection or another event shares the lookup pair. Grok never falls back
  to native lifetime totals. Missing historical account/host scope is not recovered
  from current settings. Only the old identified non-Grok native path remains for
  compatibility; unidentified legacy data and every legacy stream zero stay
  uncertain. Missing/partial contributions keep aggregate costs unknown.

Independent static review confirmed result propagation through `cue-executor`
and `cue-run-manager` without another database or serializer. Its coverage-label
finding was resolved by retaining conservative partial stream coverage.

# GT1-04-11D Canonical account discovery

Implemented on 2026-10-31 from `fd6dc1808666dfb24b5fbaee030096a231f8ff29`. Execution checkout
and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and run-state. Base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`,
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, ahead/behind **263/10**.
No ancestry changes. Unrelated `.sloptimize/` is preserved. Reviewed [[01]] source
receipts, [[02]] account-layer rules, [[03]] native identity evidence, the canonical
utility entries, provider/platform/storage guides and [[GROK-TIER1-04]].

## Implementation and boundaries

Only `src/main/agents/provider-account-dirs.ts` changes production behavior (**61 changed lines**).
The existing collector now uses the shared `resolveAgentAccountDir` with one
provider/global/process/home layer snapshot for each local agent. Explicit empty
or unrelated session env records still replace provider settings; winning blanks
still select the implicit default instead of reviving a lower account. Tilde
expansion and relative-path normalization belong to the canonical resolver.

Provider-default discovery also uses those layers when there are no local agents.
It follows sorted session selections so an implicit provider default cannot displace
a configured selection of the same real transcript pool. Independent process,
implicit-default and sibling pools remain separately discovered under their own
roots; the process candidate is normalized without borrowing higher layers.
The existing sessions-subtree realpath dedupe, recoverable error handling and
retention of missing selected roots remain intact. SSH agents are skipped before
local account resolution. The registry still controls every provider; no Grok-only
parallel discovery path was added. `globalShellEnvVars` is now available on the
existing discovery options; the single-agent options name remains a compatible alias.

Managed binding precedence in the shared resolver is unchanged. No existing
store field or host-policy getter supplies a current managed account path at this
discovery boundary. Historical native account references are opaque, not paths,
and are not reinterpreted as current selectors. As recorded in [[02]], real managed
account wiring/qualification remains with the managed-route work; this change
makes no managed-account or billing-identity claim. No account content, credentials,
provider process or SSH host was accessed.
